#   The other five groups: device, six tokens twelve generated, cpu 0.378 s
#     (0.054 + 0.323, 1.98 s processor) against device 0.259 s (0.030 +
#     0.228, 0.07 s), the part at 1701 MHz mean of 2700 at 16.6 W. kernels,
#     row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.38, q4_k 0.39, q5_k 0.41.
#     Engine, reference, drafting and outside are Q8_0 or Q2_K figures on
#     paths this change does not reach.
#
# load: 0.75 rising to 4.19 for the benchmark, which is the run's own rise;
#       0.69 to 0.81 for the end-to-end alternation; under 1.20 for the A/Bs
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-05, the weight's sign moved onto the activation, and the wall
# behind it.
#
#   llama.cpp's mul_sum_i8_pairs_float gives the byte instruction its
#   unsigned operand with VPSIGNB rather than with a bias: |w| as the
#   unsigned side, the activation signed by w as the signed side. Nothing
#   to correct afterwards. This kernel biased by a hundred and twenty-eight
#   and took a hundred and twenty-eight times the activation's block total
#   back off, which cost a whole second loop over the blocks.
#
#   PER 32-ELEMENT BLOCK       before   after
#     the dot product            10      12
#     scale and correction       18       4
#                                28      16
#
#   FIVE ALTERNATED ROUNDS, Q8_0, 64 generated after a six-token prompt:
#     before 1.726, 1.738, 1.732, 1.730, 1.731 s
#     after  1.713, 1.710, 1.708, 1.718
#   Every reading after below every reading before and the ranges do not
#   touch. One per cent. Q8_0's 110-token prompt and Q4_K generating are
#   unchanged, both being other kernels.
#
#   ONE PER CENT FOR FORTY PER CENT FEWER INSTRUCTIONS IS THE FINDING.
#   43.1e9 instructions in 39.0e9 cycles is an IPC of 1.11 on a core that
#   retires six -- stalled about eighty per cent of the time. A generated
#   token reads 1.09 GiB at about 44 GB/s and llama.cpp reads the same file
#   at about 46. Threads: 1.707, 1.723, 1.738, 1.728, 1.742 s at four, six,
#   eight, twelve and sixteen, flat from four up. The memory is saturated
#   at four workers and arithmetic is not the wall.
#
#   DIGESTS MOVE ONCE. 64 generated on the processor goes 448c2ed68ec342ee
#   to 1cb5fffbb21399ad. Accuracy is unchanged by every continuous measure
#   the suite has: conformance 28344 sequences and 0 outside tolerance
#   either way, and the fixture comparison reports the same 73 logits moved
#   by less than a disagreement either way. The f32 path answers the old
#   digest, so one argmax flips on a near-tie. The bias was adding a term
#   larger than the answer in binary32 and removing it in binary64; this is
#   different arithmetic, not worse.
#
#   The activation quantizer now clamps at -127 rather than -128, because
#   VPSIGNB cannot represent an activation of -128 (it negates to itself).
#   It could not arise -- the scale is the largest magnitude over 127 -- and
#   now it cannot arise at all. A weight of -128 is fine.
#
#   The strip kernels keep the bias: there the weights are the per-row
#   operand, so both VPSIGNBs land in the innermost loop, and the
#   correction there is already one integer add folded into the insertion.
#
#   THE COMPARISON TABLE, TAKEN AGAIN IN THIS SITTING, llama.cpp at
#   95b8e33e1, three rounds of the processor rows and two of the rest:
#
#     model_runner cpu  110 in 0.318 s (speed-prompt-110), 1419 in 5.103 s,
#       64 generated in 1.710 s
#     model_runner dev  0.070 s, 0.820 s, 1.244 s
#     llama-bench --device none  pp110 303.6, pp1419 262.9, tg64 39.4
#     llama-bench -ngl 99        pp110 1608.1, pp1419 1822.0, tg64 56.4
#     llama-bench -ngl 0         pp110 719.0, pp1419 1348.1, tg64 39.0
#
#   Which puts the processor AHEAD on both prompts -- 1.14 times on the
#   short one and 1.06 on the long one -- and 1.05 behind generating. The
#   device is 1.02 and 1.05 behind on the prompts and 1.10 generating. No
#   row in the table is more than fifteen per cent apart in either
#   direction, which this comparison has not been able to say before.
#
#   llama.cpp itself read pp110 at 298.85 and 308.43 across the two rounds
#   and pp1419 at 263.55 and 262.34, against 364.70 and 304.08 in the
#   sitting of 2026-08-29. Its own spread is what it has always been.
#
# load: under 0.90 for the A/B, under 1.10 for the comparison rows
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0

# 2026-09-05, one activation scale for two hundred and fifty-six.
#
#   llama.cpp's Q8_K exists for one reason: with one activation scale for a
#   whole two hundred and fifty-six, a k-quant's minimum term factors into
#   an integer dot product. This implements it.
#
#     with a scale for every 32:
#       Undo = sum over 8 of  dmin * min(sub) * dact(sub) * total(sub)
#     with one scale for 256:
#       Undo = dmin * dact * sum over 8 of  min(sub) * total(sub)
#
#   The inner sum is eight six-bit numbers against eight sums of thirty-two
#   bytes -- at most 63 and 4064, so eight products fit in an int32 many
#   times over. One vpmulld, a horizontal add, one convert: eight
#   instructions where the scalar loop was about fifty.
#
#   FIVE ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K_M ten generated   before 0.252 0.249 0.251 0.254 0.251 s
#                            after  0.236 0.236 0.238 0.240 0.226   1.064
#     Q5_K_M 32 generated    before 0.860 0.861 0.855 0.853 0.857
#                            after  0.837 0.840 0.848 0.850 0.802   1.020
#     Q4_K_M 110 prompt      before 0.453 0.448 0.473 0.450 0.457
#                            after  0.466 0.455 0.461 0.444 0.446   wash
#     Q8_0 64 generated      before 1.717 1.708 1.706 1.722 1.711
#                            after  1.721 1.720 1.714 1.721         wash
#
#   Five of five on the same side for Q4_K with the ranges not touching --
#   0.226 to 0.240 against 0.249 to 0.254. Q5_K gains less because its dot
#   loop is bigger. The prompt is a wash because the strip kernels do not
#   exploit it yet; Q8_0 is the control and does not take this path.
#
#   THE DIGESTS DO NOT MOVE. 4b6e8e99ae285b2a and 0a1a63f0305d35d6 before
#   and after, on a coarser activation quantization AND a restructured
#   minimum term. Conformance 28344 sequences, 0 outside tolerance either
#   way. Ten and thirty-two greedy tokens are not a sensitive instrument.
#
#   WHAT IT COSTS is a coarser activation: one scale over 256 values rather
#   than eight over 32 each. Taken only where it buys something --
#   Supers_Vectors is Q4_K and Q5_K and nothing else, so Q8_0 keeps its
#   thirty-two. Scales and Totals keep their shapes: the one scale is
#   written into all eight block slots and the sums stay per-32, so every
#   other reader is untouched.
#
#   NOT DONE: the strip kernels receive super-block activations and do not
#   use them. Their minimum term is still eight floating-point multiply-adds
#   against a per-call table, and factoring it needs the minimums kept as
#   whole numbers rather than pre-multiplied into Held_Down.
#
#   THE SITTING, at load 0.76 for the benchmark and 0.73 to 0.87 for the
#   alternation:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 2409 serial and 13064 at eight shares;
#     thirty-two a pass 4341 and 21196. q4_k one vector 2452 and 13317;
#     thirty-two 4548 and 22527. Two per cent ahead with one vector, six
#     batched, two serially -- against +3/+10/-1 and +0.4/-1/-1 in the two
#     sittings before, where the eight-bit batched figure alone went 20754,
#     23039, 21196 on code that changed once.
#
#   end to end, alternated four rounds, the default prompt: q4_k 0.294,
#     0.290, 0.286, 0.288 s for ten generated -- 24.0, 23.6, 23.5, 23.9 ms a
#     token -- against q8_0 0.368, 0.365, 0.359, 0.364 for twelve, 26.7,
#     26.8, 26.3, 26.7, and q5_k 0.369, 0.369, 0.374, 0.372 for twelve,
#     26.0, 26.2, 26.2, 26.1. The four-bit format is eleven per cent ahead
#     of the eight-bit one per token, from six.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.41, q4_k 0.40, q5_k
#     0.43, q6_k 0.41, q4_0 0.33, bf16 0.34, f32 0.28.
#
#   Engine, reference, device, drafting and outside are Q8_0 or device
#   figures on paths this change does not reach.
#
# load: 0.76 for the benchmark, 0.73 to 0.87 for the alternation, under
#       1.20 for the A/B
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-05, the strip kernels asked the same question, and answering it
# twice cost nothing and changed nothing.
#
#   The entry above says the strip kernels receive super-block activations
#   without exploiting them and names the work. Both halves of that were
#   measured. No source changed.
#
#   THE INTEGER MINIMUM TERM DOES NOT APPLY THERE, and the reason is
#   structural. The strip multiplies four vectors at once, so the four are
#   already the vector dimension: the term is three instructions for each
#   sub-block of each row -- vbroadcastss of the weight's minimum, a
#   four-wide vmulps against a per-call table, a four-wide add -- and those
#   three cover all four vectors. The integer form is three there too
#   (vpbroadcastd, vpmulld, vpaddd) and then owes a convert and a scaling
#   for each vector at the end of every block. Strictly more. The
#   single-vector kernel gained because it had no vector dimension and the
#   term was eight scalar multiply-adds.
#
#   THE CEILING, ablated rather than argued. Q4_K 1419-token prompt with
#   the minimum term's multiply removed -- wrong answer, none of it:
#
#     as it is                   6.962, 6.920 s
#     the minimum term gone      6.445, 6.484    -7.0%
#     that and the scale term    6.652, 6.724    -3.6%
#
#   Seven per cent is the whole of what any rewrite of that term could buy.
#   The third row removing more work and measuring slower is the ablation
#   losing its own vectorization, and is why an ablation is a ceiling and
#   not a plan.
#
#   AND THE LAYOUT THE PROFILE POINTED AT, TRIED AND REFUSED. The strip's
#   scale table is a sub-block at a time with the two rows interleaved, so
#   the compiler computes four sub-blocks by four vectors in one zmm
#   multiply and scatters to four addresses thirty-two bytes apart -- one
#   vmovdqa and three vextracti32x4 where one store would do. Laid out a
#   row at a time it is one store. Bit-exact, digest 1a26d24d33b8957b
#   either way, four alternated rounds:
#
#     Q4_K pp1419  interleaved 6.856, 6.944, 6.988, 6.939 s
#                  a row       6.992, 6.902, 6.896, 6.884   wash
#     Q5_K pp1419  interleaved 7.187, 7.316, 7.226, 7.178
#                  a row       7.352, 7.327, 7.293, 7.268   1.4% worse
#     Q4_K pp110   interleaved 0.450, 0.442, 0.444, 0.467
#                  a row       0.452, 0.457, 0.451, 0.449    wash
#
#   The insertion reads a sub-block's two rows back to back. Interleaved
#   they are sixteen bytes apart and in one cache line; a row at a time
#   puts them a hundred and twenty-eight apart in two. What the store side
#   saves the load side pays. Not kept.
#
# load: under 1.10 throughout
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M

# 2026-09-06, where llama.cpp's four-bit advantage is, and two of it taken.
#
#   Every figure published against llama.cpp here has been Q8_0, where this
#   program is level or ahead. Asked of Q4_K it was 1.6 to 1.7 times behind
#   on every shape. This is where that is.
#
#   HALF OF IT IS A FLAG. llama.cpp repacks Q4_K at load into block_q4_Kx8,
#   eight rows interleaved, and multiplies with ggml_gemv_q4_K_8x8_q8_K.
#   llama-cli exposes it as -nr, so it can be priced from outside:
#
#     TinyLlama Q4_K_M --device none    prompt    generating
#       llama.cpp, repacking            331.5     64.8 t/s
#       llama.cpp, -nr                  230.5     64.0
#       model_runner                    246.1     44.4
#
#   The repack is worth 1.44x on a prompt and nothing generating, and
#   without it this program's prompt is ahead. So the prompt gap is one
#   named change -- an eight-row interleaved weight layout built at load --
#   and is not built here. Named with its size, which is the rule.
#
#   THE GENERATING GAP IS THE KERNEL AND IS BIGGER THAN IT LOOKS. At one
#   thread llama.cpp reads 43.6 t/s and this program 14.1 -- three times
#   behind per core. At the worker count each chooses it is 1.47, because
#   llama.cpp saturates the memory at two threads and this program does not
#   saturate it at all.
#
#   AND THE PROFILE SAID SOMETHING SURPRISING. A single-threaded generated
#   token executes 16.7e9 instructions at IPC 3.15 -- instruction-bound, not
#   stalled -- and its hottest instructions were not in the four-bit kernel.
#   They were a sixteen-iteration scalar loop with a floating-point store an
#   iteration, at better than a fifth of everything executed, belonging to
#   Q6_K: the format a "_M" file puts on its output projection.
#
#   TWO CHANGES.
#
#   The four-bit scale kept whole, which is what ggml_vec_dot_q4_K_q8_K
#   does: vpmaddubsw for int16 pair-sums, vpmaddwd against the six-bit
#   factor held twice in a thirty-two bit word, vpaddd into an integer
#   accumulator, one convert and one multiply-add a super-block, where this
#   kernel converted and scaled each of eight sub-blocks separately.
#
#   Worth 2.3 per cent alone -- 0.239, 0.240 s before against 0.235, 0.234
#   -- and the reason it is not more is worth keeping. The argument for it
#   was the dependency chain: eight four-cycle fused multiply-adds is
#   thirty-two cycles a super-block where eight integer adds is eight. The
#   argument is wrong. Consecutive rows are independent and the reorder
#   window spans them, so the chain was never the limit; the instruction
#   count fell four in sixty and the time fell about that much.
#
#   The six-bit prologue in lanes. Sixteen halves, each a signed byte that
#   had to become a float, take two scales, be stored, and have its product
#   with the activation's half-total joined to a running sum. About ten
#   instructions a half and a hundred and sixty a block, against a dot
#   product of sixty. Twenty-six now: two vpmovsxbd, two vcvtdq2ps, a
#   broadcast multiply, the activation's eight scales permuted into the
#   sixteen the halves want, and the minimum's term as a multiply, an fmadd
#   and one eight-lane reduction.
#
#   TOGETHER, five alternated rounds, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K_M ten generated  before 0.239 0.237 0.236 0.235 0.240 s
#                           after  0.224 0.225 0.223 0.222 0.226   5.5%
#     Q5_K_M 32 generated   before 0.837 0.842 0.844 0.836 0.839
#                           after  0.813 0.810 0.810 0.809 0.816   3.5%
#     Q8_0 64 generated     before 1.715 1.717 1.717 1.719 1.714
#                           after  1.731 1.723 1.716 1.721         wash
#
#   Five of five on the same side for both k-quants with the ranges not
#   touching.
#
#   AND A FALSE ALARM WORTH RECORDING. The 110-token prompt read 0.470,
#   0.480, 0.474 against 0.447, 0.439, 0.440 in the first three rounds --
#   a five per cent regression on a path neither change touches. Eight
#   further alternated rounds:
#
#     before 0.466 0.465 0.442 0.456 0.449 0.474 0.432 0.443  median 0.4525
#     after        0.439 0.460 0.446 0.455 0.447 0.443 0.450  median 0.447
#
#   Level, four rounds each way. The base spread alone is 0.432 to 0.474,
#   ten per cent on unchanged code. Three rounds was not enough for this
#   figure and has not been all session.
#
#   DIGESTS. Q4_K's do not move. Q5_K's 32-token run goes 0a1a63f0305d35d6
#   to 647dcd7901e9779d, because a "_M" file's Q6_K tensors are in that path
#   and the minimum's term is now summed in a different order. Conformance
#   28344 sequences, 0 outside tolerance.
#
#   THE SITTING, benchmark at load 0.82 and the alternation at 0.85:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 2408 serial and 13274 at eight shares;
#     thirty-two a pass 4537 and 22702. q4_k one vector 2466 and 13384;
#     thirty-two 4364 and 22482.
#
#   end to end, alternated four rounds: q4_k 0.278, 0.275, 0.276, 0.276 s
#     for ten -- 22.6, 22.4, 22.5, 22.5 ms a token -- against q8_0 26.5,
#     26.7, 26.7, 26.6 and q5_k 25.2, 25.2, 25.7, 25.1. The four-bit format
#     is eighteen per cent ahead of the eight-bit one per token, from
#     eleven.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.40, q4_k 0.40, q5_k
#     0.43, q6_k 0.41, q4_0 0.33, bf16 0.34, f32 0.28.
#
#   outside, Q4_K: llama-bench --device none pp110 390.92 tg64 65.32
#     against this program's 246.1 and 44.4; llama-cli -nr 230.5 and 64.0.
#     Single thread, tg32: llama-bench 43.60 against 14.1.
#
# load: 0.82 for the benchmark, 0.85 for the alternation, under 1.10 for
#       the A/Bs
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the same sixteen sums, formed a thousand times.
#
#   The entry above vectorized the six-bit k-quant's weight prologue. A
#   fresh profile says the hottest instructions in the program are still a
#   sixteen-iteration scalar loop -- a different one, twenty-seven per cent
#   of everything a single-threaded generated token executes.
#
#   It is the activation's side: Q6_K wants the activation's sum over every
#   sixteen elements and formed it a byte at a time. Two things wrong, and
#   the second is larger. Sixteen signed bytes added one at a time is about
#   sixty-four instructions where twelve will do; and it depends on nothing
#   but the activation, yet lives in a kernel called once for every ROW
#   TILE, so a thirty-two thousand row output projection forms the same
#   hundred and twenty-eight sums a thousand times.
#
#   Only the first is fixed. Two vpmovsxbd, three folds, one convert, one
#   multiply, one store: twelve against sixty-four. Hoisting it out of the
#   tile would remove it rather than shrink it and is the next change on
#   this path -- not made now because it needs somewhere to live that
#   outlives the call, and every such place here is a pool buffer.
#
#   FIVE ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K_M ten generated  before 0.224 0.223 0.213 0.222 0.226 s
#                           after  0.188 0.185 0.186 0.185 0.185   17%
#     Q5_K_M 32 generated   before 0.810 0.809 0.766 0.810 0.813
#                           after  0.688 0.659 0.689 0.688 0.689   15%
#     Q4_K_M 110 prompt     before 0.441 0.431 0.437 0.444 0.453
#                           after  0.445 0.459 0.440 0.442         wash
#     Q8_0 64 generated     before 1.730 1.717 1.646 1.722 1.720
#                           after  1.734 1.646 1.720 1.716 1.727   wash
#
#   Five of five on the same side for both k-quants with the ranges not
#   touching -- 0.185 to 0.188 against 0.213 to 0.226. Digests unchanged:
#   4b6e8e99ae285b2a and 647dcd7901e9779d. Conformance 28344 sequences, 0
#   outside tolerance.
#
#   WHERE IT LEAVES THE FOUR-BIT FORMAT. 18.7 ms a token against
#   llama.cpp's 15.2 -- 1.23 times behind, from 1.58 when this began. Per
#   core 2.3 times, from 3.1. And thirty per cent faster per token than the
#   eight-bit format, which two sittings ago was eleven.
#
#   THE SITTING, benchmark at load 0.89 and the alternation at 0.76 to 0.89:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 2403 serial and 13139 at eight shares;
#     thirty-two a pass 4394 and 20974. q4_k one vector 2452 and 13360;
#     thirty-two 4415 and 20007.
#
#   end to end, alternated: q4_k 0.187, 0.187, 0.189 s for ten -- 18.7,
#     18.7, 18.9 ms a token -- against q8_0 0.318, 0.320, 0.320, 0.320 for
#     twelve (26.5 to 26.7) and q5_k 0.257, 0.254, 0.258, 0.257 (21.4,
#     21.2, 21.5, 21.4). One round refused itself on a rising load and is
#     not counted.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.40, q4_k 0.40, q5_k
#     0.43, q6_k 0.41.
#
#   outside, Q4_K: llama-bench --device none pp110 400.06 tg64 65.86
#     against this program's 248 and 53.5. Single thread tg32: llama-bench
#     43.60 against 18.9, from 14.1.
#
# load: 0.89 for the benchmark, 0.76 to 0.89 for the alternation, under
#       1.10 for the A/B
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the sixteen sums, hoisted out of the tile.
#
#   The entry above named this and said why it had not been done: the sums
#   depend on nothing but the activation, but the kernel that formed them is
#   called once for every row tile, and moving them needs somewhere to live
#   that outlives the call. The place is the quantizer.
#
#   It already walks every element and already carries a running total per
#   thirty-two; it now writes that total out at the halfway point as well.
#   One store for every thirty-two elements, once for the whole product, and
#   the kernels read the sums instead of forming them. Halves travels beside
#   Totals the whole way -- Quantize_Vectors, Quantize_Blocks,
#   Accumulate_Rows, Mat_Mul_Range_Packed, the pool's buffers, the packing
#   share -- because that is where Totals already goes.
#
#   THE FIRST ATTEMPT COST THREE TO FIVE PER CENT and is why the loop is
#   written as two of sixteen. Asking for the half-sum where it falls --
#   "if Index = Activation_Half" inside the element loop -- puts a compare
#   on every element of every activation, and it measured on paths that read
#   none of the answer:
#
#     Q8_0 64 generated   1.720 s -> 1.784   +3.7%
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt    6.958   -> 7.329   +5.3%
#
#   Two loops of sixteen with the sum taken between them is the same work
#   without the test, and both went back to level.
#
#   FIVE ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K_M ten generated  before 0.179 0.188 0.184 0.186 0.187 s
#                           after  0.189 0.183 0.183 0.183 0.183
#     Q4_K_M 1419 prompt    before 6.652 6.944 6.932 6.799 6.809
#                           after  6.863 6.788 6.801 6.857 6.882
#     Q4_K_M 110 prompt     before 0.435 0.465 0.435 0.456 0.446
#                           after  0.456 0.461 0.463 0.445 0.438
#     Q8_0 64 generated     before 1.651 1.719 1.716 1.712 1.721
#                           after  1.720 1.724 1.719 1.720 1.718
#
#   Round one is excluded from every column: its Q8_0 control reads 1.651
#   against 1.712 to 1.724 everywhere else, so that round's base was taken
#   in a faster clock window than its own new. On the other four, generating
#   is 0.183 four times out of four against 0.184 to 0.188 -- about two per
#   cent, and steadier, which is the shape to read. The work is gone rather
#   than faster, so what is left no longer varies with how many tiles a
#   tensor is cut into. The prompts are a wash.
#
#   Digests unchanged: 4b6e8e99ae285b2a, 647dcd7901e9779d,
#   cbf29ce484222325. Conformance 28344 sequences, 0 outside tolerance. The
#   conformance test that quantizes a run whole and in pieces now also
#   checks that the two halves of every block add up to its total.
#
#   THE SITTING, benchmark at load 0.83:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 2407 serial and 13303 at eight shares;
#     thirty-two a pass 4426 and 20833. q4_k one vector 2467 and 13458;
#     thirty-two 4398 and 22892.
#
#   end to end, alternated four rounds: q4_k 18.5, 18.3, 18.1, 18.2 ms a
#     token against q8_0 26.6, 26.7, 26.5, 26.6 and q5_k 21.1, 21.3, 20.9,
#     21.1. The four-bit format is thirty-one per cent ahead of the
#     eight-bit one per token.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.40, q4_k 0.41, q5_k
#     0.43, q6_k 0.41.
#
#   outside, Q4_K: llama-bench --device none pp110 385.82 tg64 65.64
#     against this program's 248.9 and 54.8 -- 1.20 times behind
#     generating, from 1.58 when this began. Single thread tg32: 43.60
#     against 18.8.

# load: 0.83 for the benchmark and the alternation, under 1.10 for the A/B
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the repack, priced and refused. No source changed.
#
#   The entry above names llama.cpp's eight-row interleaved Q4_K layout as
#   worth 1.44 times on its own prompt and leaves it unbuilt. It stays
#   unbuilt. Two mechanisms could make it pay and both were measured.
#
#   THE SEQUENTIAL WEIGHT STREAM IS WORTH ABOUT ONE PER CENT. The strip
#   kernel reads two rows a panel. Pointing the second row's weight loads at
#   the first row's bytes leaves every instruction in place and halves the
#   weight traffic -- wrong answers, and the time is the question:
#
#     Q4_K 110 prompt, one thread     two streams 2.215 s  one 2.304
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt, eight threads             6.861    one 6.775
#
#   Nothing either way. An interleaved layout that exists to make the
#   weights arrive sequentially is buying something already free here.
#
#   WHAT IT IS WAITING FOR IS INSTRUCTIONS. One thread, 110-token prompt:
#   39.81e9 instructions in 10.90e9 cycles, an IPC of 3.65. Counted against
#   llama.cpp on the same prompt at one thread, three repetitions each
#   (llama-bench also warms up, so its count covers about four passes and
#   this is a ratio rather than a reading):
#
#     llama.cpp   72.63e9 instructions, 6.30 s
#     this        117.73e9 instructions, 7.04 s
#
#   Per element-product: the byte instruction's floor is 0.031, llama.cpp
#   is at about 0.16, this program at about 0.35. Both far above the floor,
#   llama.cpp 2.2 times closer to it. The distance is scale plumbing, not
#   weight traffic.
#
#   SO THE CHANGE WORTH MAKING IS THE ONE ALREADY MADE TWICE ELSEWHERE.
#   This kernel spends four instructions on every sub-block of every vector
#   of every row -- vpxor, vpdpbusd, vcvtdq2ps, vfmadd231ps -- and carries a
#   floating-point scale table indexed by all three. Keeping the sub-block
#   factor a whole number and converting once a super-block removes the
#   convert; and in the strip kernel the factor does not depend on the
#   vector, so the table collapses to one entry per sub-block and row, a
#   quarter of its size and a quarter of the prologue that fills it.
#
#   Estimated at eleven per cent of the dot loop plus most of a prologue an
#   earlier ablation put at seven per cent of the prompt. Named rather than
#   built, under the same rule the repack was named under.
#
#   The repack may still be right for a kernel shaped like llama.cpp's
#   unrepacked one, which reads a single row at a time. This one reads two,
#   which is where the first tranche of that 1.44 went.
#
# load: under 1.20 throughout
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M

# 2026-09-06, the strip's scale, kept whole.
#
#   The entry above prices llama.cpp's repack at nothing here and names this
#   instead, estimating eleven per cent of the dot loop and most of a
#   prologue an ablation puts at seven per cent of the prompt. Built. Six and
#   a half per cent.
#
#   ONE INSTRUCTION OF THE FOUR WAS DOING NO ARITHMETIC. The innermost work
#   was vpxor, vpdpbusd, vcvtdq2ps, vfmadd231ps -- per sub-block, per row,
#   per vector. The convert is there only because the scale is floating
#   point, and it is floating point only because the weight's six-bit factor
#   was folded into the block's scale before the kernel saw it. Unfolded it
#   is a whole number no larger than sixty-three:
#
#     vpmaddubsw  activation, weights -> int16 pair sums
#     vpdpwssd    factor{1to8}, those -> int32, accumulated
#
#   Two instructions, and vpdpwssd is why: it takes a broadcast memory
#   operand, so the factor arrives free exactly as the scale used to, with
#   no separate broadcast and no separate add. Eight sub-blocks accumulate
#   whole and the super-block converts once.
#
#   AND THE TABLE SHRINKS WITH IT. The factor does not depend on the vector
#   where the folded scale did, so an entry for every sub-block, row and
#   vector becomes one for every sub-block and row plus eight for the block
#   -- sixteen numbers where there were sixty-four, filled with a quarter of
#   the arithmetic. The slot keeps its stride of sixty-four so the insertion
#   still walks it with the counter it walks the activation with.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt  before 6.738 6.872 7.011 6.917 s
#                       after  6.416 6.471 6.473 6.364      6.5%
#     Q4_K 110 prompt   before 0.443 0.452 0.442 0.446
#                       after  0.434 0.418 0.407 0.415      6.3%
#     Q5_K 1419 prompt  before 7.158 7.136 7.099 7.106
#                       after  7.163 7.118 7.151 7.108      control
#     Q4_K ten generated before 0.189 0.183 0.183 0.183
#                        after  0.191 0.184 0.184 0.183     control
#
#   Four of four on the same side for both Q4_K rows with the ranges not
#   touching. The two controls are the point: Q5_K's prompt goes through a
#   strip kernel this does not touch and Q4_K generating through the
#   single-vector one, and both stay where they were. Digests unchanged --
#   1a26d24d33b8957b and cbf29ce484222325 -- because what changed is exact
#   until the convert. Conformance 28344 sequences, 0 outside tolerance.
#
#   Q5_K's strip kernel still folds its scale. Same change again with the
#   fifth bit's plane to carry through it. Named, not built.
#
#   THE SITTING, benchmark at load 0.80:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 2418 serial and 12588 at eight shares;
#     thirty-two a pass 4490 and 20975. q4_k one vector 2393 and 13310;
#     thirty-two 4547 and 21304.
#
#   end to end, three rounds: q4_k 0.183, 0.182, 0.183 s for ten -- 18.3,
#     18.2, 18.3 ms a token -- against q8_0 0.321, 0.318, 0.319 for twelve
#     and q5_k 0.254, 0.254, 0.255.

#
#   outside, Q4_K: this program pp110 0.423 s (260.0 t/s) and pp1419 6.366 s
#     (222.9), single thread pp110 2.090 s (52.6). llama-bench --device none
#     pp110 387.08, pp1419 321.12, tg64 64.86; single thread pp110 77.74.
#     The gap per core is 1.48, from 1.58, and this program is 1.13 times
#     ahead of llama.cpp's unrepacked prompt kernel.
#
# load: 0.80 for the benchmark, under 1.10 for the A/B and the rest
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the five-bit strip's scale, kept whole.
#
#   The same change as the entry above, in the kernel that is the four-bit
#   one with a plane of fifth bits carried through the unpack. The dot is
#   the same four instructions becoming the same two -- vpmaddubsw and
#   vpdpwssd with the factor as a broadcast memory operand -- and the six
#   around it that fold the fifth bit in are untouched.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q5_K 1419 prompt   before 7.188 7.196 7.115 7.226 s
#                        after  6.603 6.512 6.569 6.565    8.7%
#     Q5_K 110 prompt    before 0.481 0.499 0.468 0.465
#                        after  0.429 0.439 0.433          8.7%
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt   before 6.441 6.572 6.430 6.432    control
#                        after  6.423 6.438 6.327 6.404
#     Q5_K 32 generated  before 0.684 0.683 0.683 0.674    control
#                        after  0.685 0.682 0.678 0.681
#
#   Four of four on the same side with the ranges not touching -- 6.512 to
#   6.603 against 7.115 to 7.226. One 110-token reading refused itself on a
#   rising load and is not counted. Both controls level: the four-bit
#   prompt goes through the kernel changed in the entry above and the
#   five-bit generated token through the single-vector one.
#
#   It is worth more here than the four-bit kernel's six and a half, because
#   the fifth bit's six instructions ride in the part that did not change,
#   so removing two of four from the part that did is a larger share of what
#   is left.
#
#   Digest 1a26d24d33b8957b unchanged, conformance 28344 sequences and 0
#   outside tolerance.

# 2026-09-06, the same instruction in the other kernel, and why it does
# nothing there. No source changed.
#
#   Recorded a commit late: the sitting it belongs to updated the README and
#   the changelog and not this file, because the edit that should have
#   written it here failed and was not noticed.
#
#   vpdpwssd took the strip kernels' innermost work from four instructions
#   to two. The single-vector kernel still had four -- vpmaddubsw, a
#   broadcast, vpmaddwd, vpaddd -- because vpmaddwd has no broadcast form.
#   Putting vpdpwssd in removes three of the four.
#
#     Q4_K generating, one thread     instructions   time
#       broadcast, multiply, add       11.84e9       0.520 s
#       one vpdpwssd                   11.31e9       0.535
#       four vpdpwssd, rotated             --        0.507
#
#   Four and a half per cent fewer instructions and three per cent slower.
#   vpdpwssd accumulates, so it is on the dependency chain at latency four
#   where vpaddd was at one: a super-block went from an eight-cycle chain to
#   thirty-two. The strip kernel has eight accumulators and does not care;
#   this one has one. Rotating four accumulators puts the chain back and
#   beats the original at one thread, and at the worker count the program
#   uses it is a wash -- five alternated rounds read 0.183 s against 0.185,
#   three better, one worse, one level. Not kept.
#
# load: under 1.10 throughout
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M

# 2026-09-06, the six-bit strip's scale, kept whole.
#
#   The largest ratio left: a thirty-two element byte product in Q6_K spans
#   two of its sixteen-element halves, which have different scales, so it
#   ended in two masked multiply-adds -- five instructions where the
#   four-bit kernel had four. vpdpwssd takes a full register operand as well
#   as a broadcast one, so a thirty-two byte entry holding one half's factor
#   in four lanes and the other's in the next four collapses all five to
#   two.
#
#   IT NEEDED Q6_K QUANTIZED AGAINST A SUPER-BLOCK FIRST. The scale that
#   comes out of the sum is the activation's, and Q6_K was the one k-quant
#   still taking a scale for every thirty-two, so there was nothing constant
#   to take out. Supers_Vectors now names it with the other two -- which is
#   what llama.cpp's Q8_K does for every k-quant and not only the two with a
#   minimum term. Conformance 28344 sequences and 0 outside tolerance either
#   way, and no digest moves.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K 110 prompt   before 0.418 0.419 0.417 0.406 s
#                       after  0.375 0.408 0.378            9.5%
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt  before 6.267 6.395 6.359 6.421
#                       after  5.992 6.075 6.042 5.926      5.6%
#     Q5_K 1419 prompt  before 6.614 6.581 6.616 6.636
#                       after  6.059 6.337 6.283 6.324      4.7%
#     Q8_0 1419 prompt  before 4.935 4.958 4.969 4.885      control
#                       after  4.935 4.934 4.933 4.987
#
#   Four of four on the same side for every k-quant row with the ranges not
#   touching. One 110-token reading refused itself on a rising load. Both
#   k-quant files gain because a "_M" file puts this format on its output
#   projection whatever its own name says, which is also why the eight-bit
#   control does not move at all.
#
#   WHERE THE FOUR PROMPTS STAND. Four-bit 292.6 tokens a second at 110 and
#   236.8 at 1419 against llama.cpp's 390 and 317 with its eight-row repack
#   -- 1.33 times behind, from 1.58 when this line of work began. Five-bit
#   267.6 and 226.5 against its 193.8 and 159.1, which is 1.38 and 1.42
#   times ahead, on the format it does not repack.
#
#   AND A BUG WORTH RECORDING. The first build ran and answered nothing: "a
#   run did not complete". The eight new integer accumulators were used by
#   the insertion and missing from its clobber list, because the edit meant
#   to add them matched a string a line break had split. The compiler is
#   entitled to keep live values in registers an insertion does not declare,
#   and did.
#
#   THE SITTING, benchmark at load 0.80: q8_0 Row_Dot 0.399 ns an element,
#   q4_k 0.406, q5_k 0.430, q6_k 0.407; q8_0 one vector 13008 at eight
#   shares and thirty-two a pass 20082, q4_k 13408 and 21042. End to end
#   q4_k 0.182, 0.183 s for ten generated, q8_0 0.320 for twelve, q5_k
#   0.254. Prompts: q4_k 0.376 s at 110 and 5.992 at 1419, q5_k 0.411 and
#   6.266.

# load: 0.80 for the benchmark, under 1.10 for the A/B and the rest
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, where the gap is after all of that. No source changed.
#
#   Nine changes in, the comparison taken again from scratch rather than
#   reasoned from the last one. Processor, TinyLlama:
#
#     Q4_K_M  110 prompt   292.6 t/s  against llama.cpp 390    1.33 behind
#             1419 prompt  236.8      against 317             1.34 behind
#             generating    54.6      against 66.5            1.22 behind
#     Q5_K_M  110 prompt   267.6      against 193.8           1.38 AHEAD
#             1419 prompt  226.5      against 159.1           1.42 AHEAD
#             generating    47.2      against 56.4            1.19 behind
#     Q8_0    generating    36.9      against 39.3            1.06 behind
#
#   The four-bit prompt rows are llama.cpp's eight-row repack, which the
#   entry above prices at one per cent of this kernel's time and which it
#   does not apply to the five-bit format -- hence the two rows ahead.
#
#   THE GENERATING ROWS ARE NOT MEMORY. A four-bit generated token moves
#   34.7 GB/s where the eight-bit one reaches 44. At one thread this program
#   reads 19.0 tokens a second against llama.cpp's 43.3 -- 2.28 times behind
#   per core -- and both run near their instruction limit:
#
#     Q4_K generating, one thread   instructions   cycles   IPC
#       model_runner, 30 tokens       33.0e9       11.6e9   2.84
#       llama.cpp, about 128          44.9e9       13.0e9   3.46
#
#   Two to three times the instructions a token at a comparable rate of
#   retiring them. The four-bit dot loop is a seventh of an instruction a
#   weight and the program spends one and a tenth, so six sevenths of them
#   are not in the arithmetic.
#
#   WHERE THEY ARE, from a profile of that single-threaded token, and none
#   of it one large thing:
#
#     the Ada loop around the per-block prologue -- counter, bound and the
#       addresses it computes for six operands of an insertion it may not
#       hoist across -- about an eighth of the kernel's instructions;
#     the six-bit output projection, which a "_M" file carries whatever its
#       own name says, at twice the instructions a weight of the four-bit
#       path;
#     the block scales, two half-precision conversions a block, and the
#       minimum's term's scalar tail between insertions.
#
#   THE NAMED NEXT CHANGE is to put the prologue's loop inside its
#   insertion, as the dot product's already is. The dot loop walks all of a
#   row's blocks with two pointer increments; the prologue is called once a
#   block from Ada and pays for the call each time. Largest single item the
#   profile shows, and the same shape as work already in this file.
#
#   Also weighed and set aside: vpdpwssd in the six-bit single-vector
#   kernel. Its dot is five instructions there against the strip's, and
#   masked accumulation would make it three -- but the weight assembly is
#   forty-eight of the block's ninety-two, so the whole change is under
#   eight per cent of that kernel and about one of a generated token.
#
# load: under 1.10 throughout
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the prologue's loop, inside the insertion.
#
#   The entry above names this as the largest single item the profile shows.
#   Built. Ten per cent per core.
#
#   The dot product has always walked a whole row inside one insertion with
#   two pointer increments; the prologue was called once a block from Ada
#   and paid for the call each time -- its counter, its bound, and the six
#   operand addresses it works out for an insertion it may not hoist across.
#   Three cursors replace them: 144 bytes a block through the weights, 32
#   through the activation's sums and scales and the factor table they fill,
#   4 through the one number a block carrying both scales multiplied.
#
#   The minimum's term is summed inside as well, in binary64 and block by
#   block, which is the order it was summed in before. Everything the
#   insertion computes it computed already and in the same order, so the
#   change is bit-exact -- 4b6e8e99ae285b2a either way, which is what checks
#   it.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q4_K ten generated, one thread    before 0.511 0.524 0.521 0.520 s
#                                       after  0.467 0.466 0.466 0.478  10.4%
#     Q4_K ten generated, eight threads before 0.190 0.183 0.184 0.182
#                                       after  0.182 0.179 0.180 0.179   2.2%
#     Q5_K 32 generated                 before 0.676 0.685 0.680 0.681  control
#                                       after  0.677 0.675 0.682 0.674
#     Q4_K 1419 prompt                  before 6.156 6.071 5.918 6.146  control
#                                       after  6.135 6.086 5.995 6.097
#
#   Four of four on the same side for both Q4_K rows and the one-thread
#   ranges do not touch. Round one's paired reading was not recorded and is
#   not counted. Both controls level: the five-bit single-vector kernel is
#   untouched and the prompt goes through the strip kernels.
#
#   TEN PER CENT PER CORE AND TWO AT EIGHT is the shape to read. The
#   instructions are gone either way; at the worker count the program uses,
#   a four-bit generated token is nearer the memory wall than the per-core
#   figure suggests -- the same thing the eight-bit kernel's sign trick
#   found. The gap to llama.cpp goes 1.22 to 1.19 at eight threads and 2.28
#   to 2.10 per core.
#
#   The same loop is still outside the insertion in the five-bit
#   single-vector kernel, which is the same change again.
#
#   THE SITTING, at load 1.11:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 12377 at eight shares, thirty-two a
#     pass 20040; q4_k one vector 13391, thirty-two 23177.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.401, q4_k 0.401,
#     q5_k 0.428, q6_k 0.406.
#
#   end to end: q4_k 0.179 s for ten generated -- 17.9 ms a token, 55.9
#     tokens a second -- q8_0 0.319 for twelve and q5_k 0.256. One thread,
#     q4_k 0.486 s for ten, which is 20.6 tokens a second against
#     llama.cpp's 43.3. Q4_K 110-token prompt 0.389 s.
#
# load: 1.11 for the benchmark and the sitting, under 1.10 for the A/B
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the same loop in the five-bit kernel, and what eight workers
# say about it.
#
#   The same change as the entry above, in the five-bit single-vector
#   kernel: two cursors -- a hundred and seventy-six bytes a block through
#   the weights and thirty-two through the activation's sums and scales and
#   the scale table they fill -- in place of an Ada loop that ran once a
#   block. Bit-exact, 647dcd7901e9779d either way.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q5_K 32 generated, one thread     before 2.028 2.019 2.026 2.035 s
#                                       after  1.919 1.897 1.894 1.856  6.5%
#     Q5_K 32 generated, eight threads  before 0.677 0.678 0.677 0.675
#                                       after  0.681 0.679 0.672 0.678  wash
#     Q4_K ten generated, one thread    before 0.471 0.474 0.474 0.472  control
#                                       after  0.471 0.469 0.469 0.471
#
#   Four of four on the same side at one thread with the ranges not
#   touching, and nothing at all at eight -- where the four-bit kernel still
#   showed two per cent. The five-bit file is 746 megabytes against the
#   four-bit's 636, so it reaches the memory wall at a lower rate of
#   arithmetic and the instructions removed have nowhere to show.
#
#   That is the second time in this section that a per-core gain has not
#   survived the worker count, and the same reason both times. Kept anyway:
#   fewer instructions for the same answers, never worse, what a run with
#   fewer workers gets, and it makes the two kernels the same shape.
#
#   THE SITTING, at load 1.49 -- above this file's own gate, so the
#   absolute numbers here are for the record rather than for comparison,
#   and the A/B above is what the claim rests on:
#
#   scaling, pinned: q8_0 one vector 13310 at eight shares and thirty-two a
#     pass 21745; q4_k one vector 13197, thirty-two 23771.
#
#   kernels, row dot in nanoseconds an element: q8_0 0.397, q4_k 0.405,
#     q5_k 0.430, q6_k 0.407.
#
#   end to end: q4_k 0.190 s for ten generated, q8_0 0.325 for twelve,
#     q5_k 0.255.
#
# load: 1.49 for the benchmark and the sitting, under 1.20 for the A/B
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the widest gap is now the device, and it is one number in the
# shader. No source changed.
#
#   With the processor's k-quant kernels done, both backends taken again.
#   The processor is level or ahead except a generated token; the device
#   prompt is 1.5 times behind and is now the widest gap in this file.
#
#     TinyLlama Q8_0                ours      llama.cpp
#       processor 1419 prompt      283.6      ~263        ahead
#       processor generating        36.7      39.3        1.07 behind
#       device 110 prompt          1279       1680        1.31 behind
#       device 1419 prompt         1210.8     1826        1.51 behind
#       device generating           50.2      56.4        1.12 behind
#     Q4_K device: 110 prompt 1196 against 1538, 1419 prompt 1214 against
#       1675, generating 74.1 against 87.6
#
#   The device prompt row has swung 1.4 times on clock state before, so it
#   was taken alternated in one window: 1.172, 1.174, 1.169 s against
#   llama-bench 1850.6, 1826.1, 1811.2, the part at 1758 to 1779 MHz
#   throughout. Not the clock this time.
#
#   BOTH SIDES USE THE SAME INSTRUCTION ON THE SAME PART. llama.cpp reports
#   "matrix cores: KHR_coopmat" and this shader has used
#   GL_KHR_cooperative_matrix at 16x16x16 since it was written. The shape
#   around it differs:
#
#                                  ours         llama.cpp mul_mm.comp
#       invocations a workgroup    64 (one)     256 (four subgroups)
#       tile a workgroup           32 x 128     64 x 64
#       tile a subgroup            32 x 128     32 x 32
#       accumulators a subgroup    16           4
#
#   AND THE REGISTER FILE SAYS WHAT THAT COSTS. RADV_DEBUG=shaderstats on
#   this program reports two pipelines at 256 vector registers -- the
#   ceiling -- and two at 168. The pair at the ceiling are the wide matrix
#   compilations with sixteen accumulators and the 168 pair the narrow ones
#   with four, which is what the accumulator count predicts: sixteen
#   accumulators is sixty-four registers before an operand fragment. At the
#   ceiling the part runs one wave to a lane group and has nothing to hide a
#   memory latency behind.
#
#   THE TILE SWEEP DID NOT LOOK HERE. It moved TILE_R, TILE_V and KCH and
#   found one free parameter already at its best. The workgroup's width is a
#   fourth constant it never touched, and the one llama.cpp differs in. A
#   narrower tile alone does not help -- that sweep measured 32x64 as five
#   per cent worse than 32x128, because a batch is a hundred and twenty-eight
#   vectors and a smaller tile is more dispatches. What is wanted is the same
#   workgroup tile over four subgroups: the dispatch count kept and the
#   accumulators quartered.
#
#   Not built. The staging loop maps sixty-four lanes onto thirty-two rows by
#   hand -- sixteen values apiece, four bytes to a word -- and spreading it
#   over two hundred and fifty-six means rewriting the decode rather than
#   changing a constant, which is the boundary that sweep drew for itself.
#
#   Also seen: a 110-token device prompt reported the part at 800 MHz for
#   its whole length, mean of 2700. That is the section on a prompt too
#   short to wake the machine, still true.
#
# load: around 1.0 to 1.5; the alternated device rounds at 1758-1779 MHz
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0 and Q4_K_M

# 2026-09-06, four subgroups in the matrix shader.
#
#   The entry above names this as the widest gap left and says the staging
#   is what stops it. It does not: the decode had to move only if all four
#   lane groups shared one chunk of columns, and they need not. Each group
#   decodes its own thirty-two columns, four chunks go into shared memory at
#   once, and all four groups then read the whole of what the four wrote.
#   The decode keeps its shape -- sixteen values to a lane, four bytes to a
#   word -- and stops being a quarter of the invocations' work.
#
#   A PROBE CAME FIRST. Four subgroups with the staging left on sixty-four
#   lanes, paying four times over on the decode: five and a half per cent
#   slower, and the matrix pipelines dropped from 168 vector registers to
#   64. Losing only five and a half while paying four times for a sixth of
#   the work is what said the rest had gained.
#
#   FOUR ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q8_0 device 1419 prompt  before 1.159 1.157 1.160 1.170 s
#                              after  1.098 1.093 1.080 1.088   5.6%
#     Q4_K device 1419 prompt  before 1.183 1.176 1.183 1.179
#                              after  1.068 1.058 1.066 1.066   9.7%
#     Q8_0 device 110 prompt   before 0.087 0.088 0.091 0.087
#                              after  0.082 0.080 0.079         8.6%
#     Q8_0 device generating   before 1.250 1.254 1.264 1.253  control
#                              after  1.252 1.284 1.245 1.207
#
#   Ranges not touching on all three prompt rows. Generating is the control
#   -- the row product, not this shader -- and stays level. Digest
#   1a26d24d33b8957b throughout. The part reported lower clocks in the
#   faster rounds, 1702 to 1729 MHz against 1761 to 1792, so the gain is not
#   the window.
#
#   WHAT IT COSTS is a wider step. The wide tile reads a hundred and twenty
#   eight columns at a time where it read thirty-two, so Tile_Step guards
#   it: a width that is not a multiple of that takes the row product for its
#   batches rather than a tile that would read past the end of a row. Every
#   hidden and feed-forward width in every model this has been shown is a
#   multiple of a hundred and twenty-eight.
#
#   WHERE IT LEAVES THE DEVICE, llama-bench in the same sitting:
#
#     device: 110 tokens 0.081 s (1358.0 t/s) against llama.cpp's 1590.9 --
#       1.17 behind, from 1.31; 1419 tokens 1.094 s (1297.1) against 1851.7
#       -- 1.43 behind, from 1.51; 64 generated 1.250 s (51.2) against 55.74
#       -- 1.09 behind. Q4_K 1419 tokens 1.066 s (1331.1) against about 1675
#       -- 1.26. Six tokens twelve generated: cpu 0.366 s against device
#       0.267 s.
#
#   Still a partial shape: four subgroups and four accumulators, and a
#   thirty-two by a hundred and twenty-eight workgroup tile where llama.cpp
#   uses sixty-four by sixty-four.
#
# load: under 1.20 throughout; the device at 1698-1792 MHz
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0 and Q4_K_M

# 2026-09-06, the sixty-four by sixty-four tile, built and refused. No
# source changed.
#
#   The other half of llama.cpp's shape. With the four subgroups already in
#   place it changes nothing about the accumulators -- two by two lane
#   groups over sixty-four rows and sixty-four vectors gives each the same
#   thirty-two by thirty-two and the same four. What it changes is traffic.
#
#   THREE ALTERNATED ROUNDS, medians of three:
#
#     Q8_0 device 1419 prompt   32x128 1.083 1.089 1.098 s
#                               64x64  1.152 1.149 1.151    5.7% worse
#     Q4_K device 1419 prompt   32x128 1.069 1.060 1.071
#                               64x64  1.104 1.118           3.9% worse
#     Q8_0 device 110 prompt    32x128 0.081 0.080 0.080
#                               64x64  0.088 0.091 0.088     10% worse
#
#   Three of three on every row and the answers right either way, digest
#   1a26d24d33b8957b.
#
#   WHY. A batch here is a hundred and twenty-eight vectors. A tile a
#   hundred and twenty-eight wide covers one in a single pass over the
#   weights; a tile sixty-four wide needs two, and the weights are what a
#   device prompt reads most of. Halving the vectors doubles the rows so the
#   activation's traffic halves -- but a model is six hundred megabytes and a
#   batch is two, and the trade is not close.
#
#   The tile sweep measured the same thing when thirty-two by sixty-four came
#   out five per cent behind thirty-two by a hundred and twenty-eight; it
#   holds with the tile twice as tall.
#
#   So llama.cpp's shape is right for llama.cpp's batching and this one for
#   this. The part that transferred was the subgroup count, which is a
#   register question and does not depend on how a batch is cut. The part
#   that did not was the tile, which is a traffic question and does.
#
#   The staging was extended for it and is not kept either: sixty-four rows
#   a chunk needs two passes of thirty-two through the decode, which works
#   and is a line of the shader that would only be read again if the batch
#   size changed.
#
# load: under 1.20 throughout
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0 and Q4_K_M

# 2026-09-06, the traffic explanation withdrawn, and where the device prompt
# really is. No source changed.
#
#   The entry above explains the sixty-four by sixty-four tile's loss by
#   weight traffic and concludes that llama.cpp's shape suits its batching
#   and this one suits this. Both halves are wrong.
#
#   ITS OWN NUMBERS REFUTE IT. A batch here is a hundred and twenty-eight
#   vectors, so a 128-wide tile passes over the weights once where a 64-wide
#   tile passes twice. That is a doubling of the largest stream a device
#   prompt reads, and it cost five and a half per cent. An argument that
#   explains a five per cent result by a hundred per cent change is not an
#   explanation. Whatever this shader waits for, it is not the weights.
#
#   AND LLAMA.CPP SETTLES IT FROM THE OTHER SIDE. Its default micro-batch is
#   512 against this program's tile of 128, and with its 64-wide tile that is
#   eight passes over the weights a micro-batch -- twenty-four for a
#   1419-token prompt against this program's twelve. It reads the model twice
#   as often and is still faster:
#
#     Q8_0 1419 device prompt      ours        llama.cpp
#       time                       1.089 s     0.767 s
#       weight bytes moved         14.0 GB     28.1 GB
#       of memory                  12.9 GB/s   36.6 GB/s
#       of arithmetic              2.68 TF/s   3.81 TF/s
#
#   The part will do about ninety gigabytes a second and the busier of the
#   two asks for thirty-seven. Neither is near the memory. The gap is the
#   rate the shader feeds its matrix instruction, and that is unresolved.
#
#   THE SIXTY-FOUR BY SIXTY-FOUR TEST DOES NOT SETTLE THE TILE. It changed
#   two things -- the tile, and the staging, which had to take two passes of
#   thirty-two rows through the decode to fill sixty-four. A result with two
#   changes in it does not attribute to one. The pair together is worse and
#   that is why the pair is not kept; the tile is not thereby shown wrong.
#
#   NAMED NEXT, rather than guessed at. This shader loads its activation
#   operand straight from global memory, column major, with the batch's
#   stride: a sixteen by sixteen fragment gathered from sixteen separate
#   lines. llama.cpp stages that operand into shared memory with coalesced
#   reads and takes the fragment from there. That is a difference in how the
#   instruction is fed, which is where the numbers above put the gap.
#
# load: figures taken from the alternated rounds above
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0

# 2026-09-06, the staging refused four ways, and the device gap withdrawn.
# No source changed.
#
#   llama.cpp fills a shared tile with its activation and loads the matrix
#   fragment from there; load_b_to_shmem takes eight halves in one
#   instruction and never fewer than two. This shader loads the fragment
#   from the batch directly. Four ways of doing it llama.cpp's way, against
#   the same alternated baseline, Q8_0 1419-token device prompt:
#
#     no staging                                    0.757 s
#     staged, 32-column window, four halves a load  0.948
#     staged, 128-column window, four halves        1.608
#     staged, 32-column window, one half a load     2.055
#     staged, 128-column window, one half           4.073
#
#   Four alternated rounds for the first pair: base 0.745 0.762 0.763,
#   staged 0.942 0.944 0.952 0.953; Q4_K base 0.745 0.737 0.751 against
#   0.971 0.968 0.977 0.984; 110-token base 0.063 0.063 0.066 against 0.083
#   0.086 0.086 0.086. Generating is the control and level. Digest
#   1a26d24d33b8957b throughout.
#
#   THE LADDER IS THE LESSON. Filling the tile a half at a time is one
#   two-byte load an invocation; four halves took it from 2.055 to 0.948, so
#   more than half the cost was the width of the load and not the copy. The
#   window matters through shared memory: 128 columns of 128 vectors is
#   thirty-four kilobytes, which leaves one group of lanes in flight where
#   nine kilobytes leaves seven.
#
#   AND THE SHAPE IS WHY IT STILL LOSES. llama.cpp stages sixty-four vectors
#   by thirty-two columns for both operands, about ten kilobytes, and pays
#   it over a sixty-four row tile. This shader would stage a hundred and
#   twenty-eight vectors and pay it over thirty-two rows: twice the tile and
#   half the rows to amortize against.
#
#   THE GAP IT WAS CHASING WAS A WINDOW. The row that started this read 1.51
#   times behind, alternated against llama-bench with the part at 1758-1779
#   MHz. Taken again the same way on the same binaries:
#
#     Q8_0 1419 device prompt    that window   this one
#       model_runner             1211 t/s      1785 t/s
#       llama.cpp                1826          1755
#
#   Ours: 0.762, 0.817, 0.795 s at 1763-1841 MHz. llama.cpp: 1752.0, 1764.9,
#   1755.1. This program's device prompt swings by half and llama.cpp's does
#   not: the same commit read 1.083-1.098 s in one sitting and 0.745-0.763 in
#   the next, and the reported clock was no lower in the slow one. What it is
#   has not been found.
#
#   So the device prompt is not a ratio but a range: ahead of llama.cpp in a
#   good window, half again behind in a bad one, and the variance is this
#   program's alone. Every relative measurement in the entries above stands,
#   each having been alternated inside its own sitting; the absolute figures
#   they quote, and the 12.9 against 36.6 GB/s and 2.68 against 3.81 TFLOP/s
#   worked out from them, were taken in a bad window and are withdrawn.
#
# load: under 1.20; the device at 1763-1841 MHz
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0 and Q4_K_M

# reason. It was cut once from this repository's own README -- code fences,
# tables and headings dropped, runs of blank lines collapsed, the first
# 60,000 bytes taken and trimmed back to a paragraph boundary -- and it must
# not be cut again: every number above is a number about that text, and a
# corpus regenerated from a later README would move all of them silently.
# What this line makes impossible is exactly that.

# and every figure in the speed section was taken again at it in one sitting

#   twelve tokens              0.593 s wall, 0.120 evaluating and 0.473

#                              back with it

# The device table, cell for cell, since the check holds every second in it

# Two of those are a change of shape rather than of number. The processor
# backend now finishes the short run before the device does and is within a

# fifth and by two and a half times -- and nothing on the device moved, so
# what a reader should take from it is that a ratio has two halves. And the
