# covers: the two tables under "The last two formats on the floating-point
#   path", the ratios, and the llama.cpp comparison beside them
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q2_K, Q3_K, Q4_0, Q4_1, Q5_0, Q5_1, Q8_0,
#        Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, the two non-linear formats in panels -- and a coverage claim
# from this morning corrected.
#
#   IQ4_NL and IQ4_XS read a nibble as an index into a table of sixteen
#   levels belonging to the format. The figures are under `### The two
#   formats whose nibble is not a number`. Both models were requantized from
#   the Q8_0 file with llama-quantize at 95b8e33e1.
#
#   THE GATHER IS ONE INSTRUCTION AND THAT DECIDES THE LAYOUT. vpshufb looks
#   up sixteen bytes by sixteen indices within each half of a register,
#   which is exactly a table of this size, so the panels keep NIBBLES rather
#   than levels: storing the levels would be a byte an element, twice the
#   room, to save one instruction. IQ4_NL needed no new layout at all --
#   its block is Q4_0's shape, so it takes Q4_0's panel and permutation
#   unchanged and only the kernel knows the difference.
#
#   Alternated three rounds against three, --backend cpu:
#
#     IQ4_NL              as stored              --repack rows        ratio
#       prompt, 110   4.082 3.956 4.036     0.188 0.182 0.182          22.1
#       prompt, 1419  55.854               3.046                       18.3
#       generating 64 7.331 7.334 7.341     1.082 1.080 1.084           6.78
#
#     IQ4_XS              as stored              --repack rows        ratio
#       prompt, 110   4.034 3.963 3.964     0.196 0.196 0.193          20.5
#       prompt, 1419  55.518               3.218                       17.3
#       generating 64 5.798 5.830 5.840     1.035 1.034 1.037           5.62
#
#   Digests unchanged at 110 on both paths -- 7f66610877cf2132 and
#   d94a8a849153bad2 -- and at 1419 IQ4_XS agrees both ways
#   (60d3652af740a25e) while IQ4_NL does not (d49de870d84a26ca stored). Two
#   arithmetics; the kernels are held by the test, not the digest.
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1, eight threads, same files, same sitting:
#   IQ4_NL pp110 474.81, pp1419 405.66, tg64 71.87; IQ4_XS pp110 190.77,
#   pp1419 163.68, tg64 73.43. So 1.27 and 1.15 times ahead for IQ4_NL and
#   2.99 and 2.69 for IQ4_XS, generating 1.21 and 1.19 behind.
#
#   IQ4_NL IS THE SECOND FORMAT WHERE BOTH SIDES REPACK, after Q2_K, AND THE
#   ONE WHERE THE TWO ANSWERS DIFFER VISIBLY. llama.cpp's is
#   block_iq4_nlx4 -- FOUR rows interleaved -- where three of its four
#   repacks (block_q4_0x8, block_q4_Kx8, block_q2_Kx8) are eight. So 1.27 is
#   eight rows against four on the same format with no asterisk. IQ4_XS is
#   not on that list and carries the caveat Q4_1, Q5_0, Q5_1 and Q3_K do.
#
#   THE KERNEL TABLE STILL DOES NOT MOVE. IQ4_NL and IQ4_XS read 0.620 and
#   0.499 ns an element there, unchanged, because Row_Dot is the row product
#   and still does the gather it always did. Fourth time in this run of
#   sections that has had to be said.
#
#   A COVERAGE CLAIM FROM THIS MORNING IS CORRECTED. The entry above says
#   `--repack rows` covers "every one the engine can multiply" and counted
#   nine. The engine reads sixteen: three are floating-point and have no
#   blocks to interleave, Q8_0 is left out on purpose, and MXFP4 simply had
#   no panel and still has none. Eleven of sixteen, with the exclusions
#   named, in both the README and the changelog. MXFP4 is IQ4_NL's shape
#   with a power-of-two scale in a byte where the half is.
#
#   HELD BY THE SAME TEST, WIDENED AGAIN. Panel_Quantized_Kernels_Are_Exact
#   covers eight kernels now. For these two the varied matrix is what
#   matters: a flat one indexes a single level of the sixteen and a varied
#   one indexes most of them, so a lookup reading the wrong entry fails on
#   the second and not the first. The eight agree to 6E-6, and it was
#   checked by breaking it -- reading the table one byte off fails by 48674.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS CONFIRMED, ONE RE-MEASURED. Confirming pairs, all inside:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.344 s against 0.348
#     device, the same run              0.241 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.289 and 4.319 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.691 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.040 + 0.101 and 5.565 + 3.647
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.338 against 1.320
#     q4km 110 and 1419 rows            0.221 and 3.566 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and the spread noted below
#     q5km 110 rows                     0.229 against 0.228-0.232
#     q2_k and q4_0 110 rows            0.247 and 0.183 against 0.251-0.261
#                                       and 0.177-0.180
#     Row_Dot                           q8_0 0.370, iq4nl 0.620,
#                                       iq4xs 0.499 -- within two per cent
#
#   The panels table's 1419-token rows cell reads 3.566 s here, a sixth
#   reading: 3.774-3.821 published, then 3.517, 3.549, 3.705, 3.585, 3.566.
#
# covers: the two tables under "The two formats whose nibble is not a
#   number", the ratios, and the llama.cpp comparison beside them
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat IQ4_NL, IQ4_XS, Q2_K, Q4_0, Q8_0, Q4_K_M and
#        Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, the block-exponent format in panels -- the last one, and the
# first that does not pay.
#
#   MXFP4 is IQ4_NL's block with a different table and a scale that is an
#   E8M0 exponent byte. The figures are under `### The block-exponent
#   format, the last one`. `--repack rows` now covers twelve formats.
#
#   THE MODEL IS A MIXTURE AND THAT HAD TO BE ARRANGED. llama-quantize
#   writes MXFP4 only for a mixture's expert tensors and TinyLlama has none,
#   so the file was made by naming each weight matrix with --tensor-type:
#   154 tensors MXFP4, the embedding and output projection left Q8_0.
#   llama-bench labels the file Q8_0 for that reason.
#
#   Alternated three rounds against three, --backend cpu:
#
#                       as stored              --repack rows        ratio
#     prompt, 110   3.968 4.025 4.214     0.261 0.264 0.268          15.4
#     prompt, 1419  57.221               4.118                       13.9
#     generating 21 2.346 2.344 2.367     0.362 0.363 0.363           6.47
#     generating 64 7.404                1.238                        5.98
#
#   Digests unchanged on both paths at both lengths: 323e0f4ae1822664 and
#   00f047036c93974f.
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1, eight threads, same file, same sitting:
#   pp110 483.11, pp1419 418.42, tg64 73.00. So 421.5 against 483.1 and
#   344.6 against 418.4 -- 1.15 and 1.21 times BEHIND, with the generated
#   token 1.41 behind.
#
#   THIS IS THE FIRST OF THE EIGHT FORMATS PANELLED TODAY WHERE THE PANEL
#   PATH LOSES, and llama.cpp does not repack MXFP4 either -- it is not in
#   repack.cpp's list -- so it is an eight-row layout losing to a row-major
#   kernel. Two candidates and neither was chased: the panel is 160 bytes
#   where the block is 136, eighteen per cent more to read and the only
#   32-element panel here that grows at all; and the scale is four bytes a
#   row where every other format's is two, thirty-two bytes a block of load
#   against sixteen. The fix, if it is one, is to keep the exponent byte and
#   convert it in the kernel -- three instructions a block against sixteen
#   bytes -- and the two subnormal exponents are what stands in the way.
#
#   THE SCALE IS WHY THE PANEL GROWS. Two to the byte less 128 runs from
#   2^-128 to 2^127 and a half holds neither end, so this is the one layout
#   here whose scales are binary32. The conversion is the decoder's bit
#   pattern rather than a power taken at run time, so it is exact, and the
#   kernel loads eight floats where every other widens eight halves.
#
#   AND THE BIAS COMES OFF IN WHOLE NUMBERS HERE where IQ4_NL takes it off
#   as a floating-point term. This format's levels reach twelve where the
#   bias is 128, so the biased byte is about ten times the number it stands
#   for and two floating-point terms of that size cancel down to the answer;
#   IQ4_NL's reach 127 and its two terms are the size of their difference.
#   Taken off before the conversion this kernel is EXACTLY equal to the
#   floating-point path on the flat matrix -- nought, the only one of nine
#   that reads zero.
#
#   THE TEST'S BOUND IS AGAINST THE SIZE OF THE ANSWERS NOW, not absolute.
#   Two things made the old bound wrong once a ninth format arrived: these
#   formats do not produce numbers of the same size, so an absolute bound
#   set by the quietest of eight is loose for one and tight for another;
#   and the varied matrix is signed, so its five hundred and twelve terms
#   cancel and dividing by a particular answer measures the cancellation
#   rather than the kernel. Dividing by the largest answer of the comparison
#   asks what is meant. The nine read between nought and 4E-5, the loudest
#   being Q4_0 -- the only one whose row-major side is an integer kernel
#   too, so its comparison is two integer kernels grouping their blocks
#   differently rather than one against the floating-point path.
#
#   AN HOUR WAS SPENT CHASING A MEASUREMENT ARTEFACT AND IT IS WORTH
#   WRITING DOWN. MXFP4 first failed the test at 1.5625E-02 against an
#   absolute bound of 1.0E-2, which was read as a kernel fault; the bias was
#   moved into whole numbers in FOUR kernels on that reading. It was not a
#   fault -- the bound was absolute and this format's answers are larger.
#   The four shipped kernels were then checked against the tightened test by
#   building the published commit: Q4_0 8.6E-08, Q5_0 8.6E-08, IQ4_NL
#   2.1E-07. NOTHING PUBLISHED EARLIER TODAY WAS INACCURATE, and the three
#   were left as they were. Only MXFP4 keeps the integer form, for the
#   reason above, which is its own and measurable.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS CONFIRMED, ONE RE-MEASURED. Confirming pairs, all inside:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.345 s against 0.348
#     device, the same run              0.246 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.286 and 4.406 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.694 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.040 + 0.103 and 5.521 + 3.642
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.341 against 1.320
#     q4km 110 and 1419 rows            0.209 and 3.671 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and the spread noted below
#     q5km 110 rows                     0.242 against 0.228-0.232
#     iq4nl 110 rows                    0.185 against 0.182-0.188
#
#   The panels table's 1419-token rows cell reads 3.671 s here, a seventh
#   reading: 3.774-3.821 published, then 3.517, 3.549, 3.705, 3.585, 3.566,
#   3.671.
#
# covers: the table under "The block-exponent format, the last one", the
#   ratios, and the llama.cpp comparison beside it
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat MXFP4 (mixed, see above), Q8_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M,
#        Q5_K_M and IQ4_NL


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, MXFP4's panel keeps the exponent byte -- and the guess the
# entry above made about the prompt was wrong.
#
#   The panel was written with binary32 scales, four bytes a row, which made
#   it the one panel over a thirty-two element block that did not fit the
#   rows it held: 160 bytes against 136. It keeps the byte now and the
#   kernel converts it -- widen the eight, shift the biased exponent into
#   place, shift the leading bit for the two subnormal exponents, compare,
#   blend under a mask; six instructions a block.
#
#   Alternated, --backend cpu, the same file as the entry above:
#
#                    binary32 scales        the exponent byte      ratio
#     prompt, 110   0.261 0.264 0.268     0.262 0.264 0.265        a wash
#     prompt, 1419  4.118                 4.134                    a wash
#     generating 21 0.362 0.363 0.363     0.325 0.326 0.326         1.11
#     generating 64 1.238                 1.125                     1.10
#     loading       0.330                 0.289
#
#   Digests unchanged at both lengths: 323e0f4ae1822664 and
#   00f047036c93974f.
#
#   THE ENTRY ABOVE GUESSED WRONG AND THIS IS THE CORRECTION. It named the
#   panel's eighteen per cent of extra bytes as the likely reason this was
#   the only kernel to lose to llama.cpp, and said keeping the exponent byte
#   would be the fix. Keeping it closed a tenth of the GENERATING gap and
#   NONE of the prompt gap. So the extra bytes were never what the prompt
#   was waiting for, and the prompt gap stands unexplained: llama.cpp reads
#   this format row-major, without a repack, and still evaluates a prompt
#   one and a sixth faster than an eight-row panel does. It is left
#   unexplained rather than dressed up.
#
#   WHAT THE PAIR DOES SAY is what a prompt and a generated token are.
#   Fifteen per cent less to read is worth a tenth of a generated token and
#   nothing at all on a prompt: one vector against the whole matrix is a
#   read, eight vectors against one reading of it is arithmetic. The six
#   conversion instructions are free on the prompt for the same reason the
#   smaller panel is -- neither was ever the thing being waited for.
#
#   Against llama.cpp now: 416.7 t/s against 483.1 at 110 tokens, 343.2
#   against 418.4 at 1419, and 56.9 against 73.0 generating -- 1.16, 1.22
#   and 1.28 behind, the last from 1.41.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS CONFIRMED, ONE RE-MEASURED. Confirming pairs, all inside:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.346 s against 0.348
#     device, the same run              0.246 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.291 and 4.592 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.690 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.040 + 0.101 and 5.535 + 3.637
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.329 against 1.320
#     q4km 110 and 1419 rows            0.220 and 3.595 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and the spread below
#     q5km 110 rows                     0.220 against 0.228-0.232
#     iq4nl 110 rows                    0.189 against 0.182-0.188
#
#   The panels table's 1419-token rows cell reads 3.595 s here, an eighth
#   reading: 3.774-3.821 published, then 3.517, 3.549, 3.705, 3.585, 3.566,
#   3.671, 3.595. Seven of the eight are below the published range and the
#   published one has never been retaken; it is the cell that should be
#   re-measured next time this group is, rather than confirmed again.
#
# covers: the table under "The block-exponent format, the last one", the
#   pair of layouts measured against each other, and the llama.cpp
#   comparison beside them
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat MXFP4 (mixed), Q8_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and
#        IQ4_NL


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, why MXFP4's prompt was behind: two arrays at one address.
#
#   The entry above left it unexplained and named the panel's size as the
#   likely cause. That was the second wrong guess. The answer is aliasing in
#   the Ada that fills the kernel's band table, and it is worth 1.47 times
#   of this format's prompt.
#
#   HOW IT WAS FOUND, because the route matters more than the answer.
#
#   First, a model matched tensor for tensor. The MXFP4 file is a mixture --
#   154 four-bit tensors, the embedding and output Q8_0 -- so an IQ4_NL file
#   was built the same way with the same --tensor-type list. Same shapes,
#   same mixture, same kernel shape, one format apart:
#
#     MXFP4   0.265, 0.270 s      IQ4_NL   0.177, 0.182 s
#
#   That says the fault is in the kernel and not in the file.
#
#   Second, an instruction count. perf stat over the same work: 25.54
#   billion instructions for MXFP4 against 17.59 for IQ4_NL, at about the
#   same instructions a cycle. The two kernels' assembly bodies are 176 and
#   167 operations -- five per cent apart. So eight billion instructions
#   were somewhere the assembly did not account for.
#
#   Third, perf record -e instructions by symbol: the kernel's own procedure
#   is 67.98 per cent of MXFP4's instructions against 48.39 of IQ4_NL's,
#   which is 17.36 billion against 8.51 -- 2.04 times, for a body five per
#   cent larger. And perf annotate put the hot instructions on `vmovss` and
#   `movq` with large displacements: the ADA band fill, not the insertion.
#
#   WHAT IT IS. The insertion reads sixteen words a block, eight activation
#   scales and eight whole-number corrections, and Ada writes that as a
#   floating-point array with an integer array imported at its address. Four
#   panel kernels here do exactly that. A write through one is a write the
#   compiler must assume changed the other, so what it knew about the
#   address arithmetic is discarded and done again.
#
#   WHY ONLY THIS ONE PAYS, and this was measured rather than reasoned:
#
#     Q4_K    20 words per 256 elements, floats then integers in separate
#             loops                                          costs nothing
#     MXFP4    2 words per  32 elements, float and integer
#             interleaved in the same iteration              costs 1.47x
#
#   Q4_K was given the same one-table treatment and reads 0.212 and 0.222 s
#   against 0.209 to 0.222 unchanged -- a wash -- so it was reverted and
#   left as it was. The fill's FREQUENCY and its ORDER are what decide this,
#   not the overlay itself.
#
#   THE FIX is one array of whole numbers with the scale written as its bit
#   pattern. The insertion reads it as a float either way and was never
#   told.
#
#     110-token prompt    0.262 0.264 0.265  ->  0.178 0.180 0.182   1.47
#     1419-token prompt   4.134             ->  3.185                1.30
#     generating 21       0.325 0.326 0.326  ->  0.321 0.323 0.323   a wash
#     generating 64       1.125             ->  1.115                a wash
#
#   Generating is unchanged and should be: its fill runs once per block for
#   ONE vector rather than eight, so the aliasing had eight times less to
#   spoil. Digests unchanged, 323e0f4ae1822664 and 00f047036c93974f.
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1, eight threads, same file, on a settled
#   machine: pp110 485.21, pp1419 404.25, tg64 72.96. So MXFP4 is now 1.26
#   and 1.10 times AHEAD on the two prompts where it was 1.16 and 1.22
#   behind, and EVERY ONE OF THE TWELVE PANELLED FORMATS IS AHEAD OF
#   LLAMA.CPP ON A PROMPT.
#
#   ONE LLAMA-BENCH READING WAS DISCARDED AND IS RECORDED HERE. Taken
#   immediately after a build, it gave pp1419 206.91 and tg64 56.73 against
#   404.25 and 72.96 on a settled machine. The load gate this file's
#   readings pass through does not cover llama-bench, which is run by hand;
#   it should be, and until it is, a reading of the other side taken while
#   the machine is busy is worth nothing.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS CONFIRMED, ONE RE-MEASURED. Confirming pairs, all inside:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.345 s against 0.348
#     device, the same run              0.244 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.292 and 4.443 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.689 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.042 + 0.101 and 5.484 + 3.626
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.354 against 1.320
#     q4km 110 and 1419 rows            0.222 and 3.573 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and the spread below
#     q5km 110 rows                     0.217 against 0.228-0.232
#     iq4nl 110 rows                    0.182 against 0.182-0.188
#
#   The panels table's 1419-token rows cell reads 3.573 s here, a ninth
#   reading: 3.774-3.821 published, then 3.517, 3.549, 3.705, 3.585, 3.566,
#   3.671, 3.595, 3.573. Eight of the nine are below the published range.
#   That cell is due a re-measurement rather than a tenth confirmation.
#
# covers: the table under "The block-exponent format, the last one", the two
#   layouts measured against each other, the aliasing pair, and the
#   llama.cpp comparison beside them
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading; llama-bench under 0.60
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat MXFP4 (mixed), IQ4_NL (mixed, matched), Q8_0,
#        Q2_K, Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M and IQ4_NL


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, llama-bench goes through the load gate now, and the gate has
# one copy again.
#
#   The entry above says a llama-bench reading taken straight after a build
#   gave pp1419 206.91 where a settled machine gave 404.25, and that the
#   load gate this file's readings pass through does not cover llama-bench.
#   It does now. `tests outside` waits for the machine exactly as `tests
#   speed` and `tests benchmark` do, runs whatever it is handed, and prints
#   the load at both ends:
#
#     tests outside --wait 10 -- llama-bench -m MODEL -p 110,1419 ...
#
#   It reads none of the output. What is gated is when somebody else's tool
#   runs, not what it says.
#
#   AND THE GATE HAD ALREADY BECOME TWO COPIES, which is what Host_Load's
#   own first paragraph warns about: "three copies of it would be three
#   things to keep in step and two of them would drift". `speed` waited or
#   refused in one set of words and `benchmark` did the same thing in
#   another. Adding a third caller was the moment to notice. Host_Load.Settle
#   is the one copy now and all three take it from there; the behaviour of
#   the first two is unchanged, which is what the confirming pairs below are
#   for.
#
#   TWO SMALLER THINGS THE GATE ITSELF CAUGHT, both real:
#
#     The usage listing had been silently short. Tool_Commands.Usage_Line
#     built it in a fixed thousand-and-twenty-four character buffer and
#     dropped whatever did not fit, so `tests` had stopped listing its
#     commands after `external-model` -- for as long as `speed` has carried
#     eighteen options, that one line being a third of the room. What goes
#     missing is the end of a list, which looks like the end of a list. It
#     is grown as it is written now.
#
#     And the option checker refused the other tool's own options on its
#     behalf, because it read every argument against this command's list. It
#     stops at a bare "--" now, which is the only place one appears.
#
#   FOUR GROUPS CONFIRMED, NONE RE-MEASURED, and the reason is what changed:
#   tests/src/benchmarks.adb is in all four and what changed in it is the
#   gate, which runs BEFORE anything is measured and cannot reach a figure.
#   The confirming pairs, all inside:
#
#     q8_0 and q4_0 Row_Dot   0.368 and 0.302 against 0.37 and 0.30
#     shares, q8_0 one vector 2627 at one share against 2630
#     device, twelve tokens   0.243 s against 0.242
#     q8 110, cpu             0.295 s against 0.286; generating 1.691
#                             against 1.689
#     q8 1419, device         0.697 s against 0.713
#     llama-bench, q8_0       pp110 390.87 against a published 397.6 and
#                             tg64 40.93 against 40.7 -- taken through
#                             `tests outside`, which is the first published
#                             reading of the other side to pass a bound
#
# load:  under 1.10 before every reading; llama-bench through the gate
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, the worker handover taken off the lock -- and the belief that
# prompted it was wrong in the way that matters.
#
#   llama.cpp's ggml_barrier (ggml-cpu.c:575) is a pure atomic spin, so its
#   threads never park inside a graph. Measured on the same Q4_0 file,
#   generating: 43 voluntary context switches against this program's 2,327,
#   and 7.0 cores hot against 5.3. The figures are under `### The handover
#   taken off the lock`.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. The job travels in the wake signal now, written before
#   the ticket that publishes it, and a worker that finds work by spinning
#   touches no lock at all -- where it used to spin and then call a
#   protected entry whether the spin had found anything or not, and report
#   through a second protected call. What is left of the blocking path is a
#   suspension object a worker: it announces that it is going to sleep,
#   re-reads the ticket, and sleeps only if nothing arrived in between,
#   while the poster raises the ticket and then reads the announcement.
#   Either the worker sees the job or the poster sees a worker to wake; both
#   costs a wake nobody needed, neither is impossible by construction.
#
#   Alternated three rounds against three, Q4_0 in panels, --backend cpu,
#   twenty-eight generated tokens:
#
#                        old handover        on the wake signal      ratio
#     generating     0.435 0.434 0.433     0.428 0.426 0.428          1.6%
#     processor      2.31 2.31 2.29        2.31 2.29 2.32             a wash
#     voluntary switches   2,327                 232                  10x
#
#   Every reading of one arm below every reading of the other, digest
#   adb365246e4b76ef throughout.
#
#   AND THE BELIEF THAT PROMPTED IT WAS WRONG. The handover was thought to
#   be what capped the generating team at five shares. It was what stopped
#   the shares being AVAILABLE -- the sweep taken again on the new pool:
#
#     five    0.424 s generating, 2.30 s processor, 5.1 cores hot
#     six     0.423 s,            2.70 s,           6.1
#     eight   0.415 s,            3.48 s,           7.9
#
#   -- eight now reaches 7.9 cores where the old pool could not pass 5.1,
#   which says the handover really was what stopped them. But being there is
#   worth TWO PER CENT of the wall for FIFTY-ONE PER CENT more processor,
#   which is the same trade five was chosen on and is refused for the same
#   reason. Vector_Team stays at five.
#
#   THIS SETTLES `### Generating is at the memory wall` FROM THE OTHER SIDE.
#   That section argued the case from a bandwidth ceiling and a per-core
#   comparison, and the standing objection was that the pool might simply be
#   unable to use the cores. It can now. Using them buys two per cent.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS CONFIRMED, NONE RE-MEASURED. Every published figure is
#   inside its range and the generating ones are slightly better, which is
#   the 1.6 per cent above rather than a figure that moved:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.342 s against 0.348, of which
#                                       generating 0.302 against 0.305
#     device, the same run              0.244 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.286 and 4.317 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.684 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.041 + 0.101 and 5.562 + 3.662
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.321 against 1.320
#     q4km and q5km 110 rows            0.218 and 0.218 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and 0.228-0.232
#     q8_0 Row_Dot                      0.372 against 0.37
#     shares, q8_0 one vector           14530 Me/s at eight against 14436
#
# load:  under 1.05 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q4_0, Q8_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, gpt-oss read: six groups confirmed, none re-measured.
#
#   The architecture MXFP4 exists for is implemented -- attention sinks, a
#   clamped gate, an alternating window on a base of its own, and biases on
#   a mixture's router and experts. What that puts on every other model's
#   path is one thing: Softmax is now two entry points onto one procedure,
#   with a Boolean saying whether a sink joins the denominator. Everything
#   else is behind `Sinks /= null` or `Gate_Alpha > 0.0` and cannot be
#   reached by an architecture that states neither.
#
#   So the question these pairs answer is whether that Boolean costs
#   anything on the path every model takes. It does not:
#
#     twelve tokens, six-token prompt   0.346 s against 0.348
#     device, the same run              0.247 s against 0.242
#     q8 110 and 1419, cpu              0.288 and 4.413 against 0.286, 4.510
#                                       generating 1.683 against 1.689
#     four tokens, cpu and reference    0.041 + 0.101 and 5.576 + 3.675
#                                       against 0.042 + 0.102, 5.611 + 3.671
#     drafting, the two-bit model       1.310 against 1.320
#     q4km and q5km 110 rows            0.208 and 0.226 against 0.212-0.216
#                                       and 0.228-0.232
#
#   Every digest unchanged.
#
#   THE ARCHITECTURE IS HELD BY A COMPARISON AND NOT BY A RUN, which is what
#   this file's rule about plausible wrong answers asks for. Dropping the
#   sink moves a logit by 0.104 and using the ordinary gate by 0.898, both
#   of which are answers rather than failures: the engine would produce
#   them and only the independent implementation of the same two rules says
#   they are wrong. Both were measured by breaking the engine on purpose.
#
#   AND IT IS NOT YET READ FROM A PUBLISHED FILE. Every other architecture
#   here has been loaded from something somebody else wrote; this one has
#   not, because no dense MXFP4 model is to hand. The support matrix says
#   so in its own row rather than leaving the omission to be noticed.
#
# load:  under 1.05 before every reading
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, what a generated token does on one task while four watch.
#
#   The section above this one settled that generating is at the memory
#   wall. It is -- for the products. Every job the pool posts was timed
#   from the outside on a 128-token Q4_0 run in panels, and what came out
#   is that 89.2 per cent of a token is inside a product, 8.5 per cent is
#   between one and the next, and 2.2 per cent is preparing one before the
#   workers are told. The 89.2 reads 599 MB in 13.3 ms, which is 45.1 GB/s
#   against the 45.1 this file measures as the ceiling; llama.cpp does the
#   whole token in 13.7 ms. So the gap is the eleven per cent on one task.
#
#   THREE CHANGES, AND THE THIRD WAS WRONG THE FIRST TIME.
#
#   The wake was measured rather than assumed: 0.69 microseconds from the
#   poster's stamp to the worker reading the ticket, timed inside the
#   worker over 14,275 jobs, and about a microsecond more at the barrier.
#   Inline_Floor was a million elements on the old assumption of tens of
#   microseconds and is 65,536, which is what lets a token's attention go
#   to the pool. Sweeps at 262,144, 16,384 and 4,096 measured 63.2, 64.4
#   and 64.4 tokens a second against 64.4 here, so the bound is chosen
#   where it stops paying rather than at the smallest number that works.
#
#   A shared job is cut into as many shares as the last product was. Five
#   of eight workers are on a generated token's products, so the other
#   three park; asking for the whole pool wakes them, and the barrier of a
#   token's attention read 22,721 ticks while it did and 3,150 once it
#   stopped -- 6.9 microseconds against 1.0.
#
#   NAMING THE VECTOR TEAM THERE WAS WRONG AND THE PROMPT SAID SO. The
#   first version asked for that team always, and the 1419-token Q8_0
#   prompt read 5.057, 4.955, 5.082 s against 4.583, 4.551, 4.658 -- nine
#   per cent, every round of three alternated. A batched product takes the
#   whole pool and the activation it packs is real work rather than a wake.
#   Following the last product instead reads 4.663, 4.562, 4.615 against
#   4.607, 4.645, 4.661: a wash. THE FIRST ARRANGEMENT WOULD HAVE SHIPPED
#   IF THE PROMPT HAD NOT BEEN ALTERNATED, because the generating figures
#   it was built for all improved.
#
#   And the activation is packed in one pass: the block loaded once into
#   two 512-bit registers, its scale, its bytes and its two sums out of
#   them. Ablating the pass it replaced -- the wide extent walk -- takes
#   packing from 13,620 ticks a call to 6,454, so that walk was more than
#   half of it; the fused insertion reads 9,955.
#
#   THE ARITHMETIC IS HELD BY EQUALITY AND NOT A TOLERANCE. A quantized
#   activation is a byte. The test packs the same run through the baseline
#   compilation and the deep one and asserts every byte, every scale, every
#   total and every half is the same, over values holding each half from a
#   half to nine and a half, both signs, a block of zeros, and the two
#   floats either side of a half. Breaking the tie on purpose fails it by
#   one assertion and dropping the finiteness test by four. THE CLAMP IS
#   NOT COVERED AND CANNOT BE: the scale is the largest magnitude over 127,
#   so nothing reaches 128 and mutating the clamp changes no answer.
#
#   WHAT IT IS WORTH. Alternated three rounds against three, 128 tokens
#   generated, --repack rows, --backend cpu; every reading of one arm
#   outside every reading of the other:
#
#     Q4_0     62.93 63.00 63.00  ->  64.83 65.00 65.08   1.032
#     Q4_K_M   52.57 52.60 52.72  ->  53.70 54.40 54.41   1.026
#     Q8_0     38.16 38.19 38.23  ->  38.83 38.99 38.99   1.020
#
#   EIGHT GROUPS RE-MEASURED, NONE CONFIRMED, because the change is in the
#   backend and the integer kernels and those are in every group's
#   fingerprint. This sitting, both sides through the same load gate:
#
#     engine 6-tok 12gen cpu     0.344 s -- 0.042 + 0.302, 1.83 s processor
#     engine, --arith f32        1.079 s, 8.62 s processor
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.244 s -- 0.028 + 0.215, 0.08 s processor
#     outside q8 110 cpu         0.289 prompt, 1.637 generating
#     outside q8 1419 cpu        4.615 prompt, 1.804 generating
#     outside q8 110 device      0.070 prompt, 1.217 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.719 prompt, 1.338 generating
#     panels q4_0 110 rows       0.170 prompt, 0.260 for seventeen
#     panels q4km 110 rows       0.213 prompt, 1.190 for sixty-four
#     panels q5km 110 rows       0.228 prompt, 1.358
#     panels q2k 110 rows        0.253 prompt, 0.906
#     drafting q2 12tok          1.321 s; drafted 2.879 s, 24 proposed 7 taken
#     reference 4tok cpu         0.043 + 0.101
#     reference 4tok reference   5.659 + 3.765
#     kernels q8_0 Row_Dot       0.372 ns an element, unchanged
#     scaling q8_0 eight shares  14,437 Me/s against 14,436 published
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1 in the same sitting, through tests outside:
#
#     q8_0 cpu     pp110 392.80  pp1419 312.42  tg64 40.61
#     q8_0 device  pp110 1665.92 pp1419 1999.31 tg64 58.74
#     q4_0 cpu     pp110 499.02  pp1419 398.42  tg64 72.24
#
#   THE MACHINE IS ABOUT ONE AND A HALF PER CENT SLOWER THAN THE SITTING
#   THAT PUBLISHED THE TABLE, and llama.cpp's own rows say so: 392.8 and
#   312.4 against 397.6 and 319.3 published. Which is what taking both
#   sides in one sitting is for, and why the generated token is the row
#   worth reading -- 39.1 against 40.6, 1.04 behind, where it was 1.07.
#
# load:  under 1.50 before every reading, both sides through tests outside
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q4_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-08, the element-wise work of a layer, on the pool.
#
#   The entry above leaves a generated token 91 per cent inside a product,
#   6.1 per cent between one and the next and 2.2 per cent preparing one.
#   This is about the 6.1, and the rule that decides all of it is whether
#   the work can be cut into ranges: element-wise work can and a reduction
#   cannot. The gated middle and the residual add are the first; a
#   normalization, which reads the sum of the whole vector before it writes
#   any of it, is the second and stays where it was.
#
#   THE BOUND, SWEPT ON THE FINAL CODE IN ONE SITTING. TinyLlama Q4_0 in
#   panels, 128 generated, three runs each, medians:
#
#     Inline_Floor  1_048_576   63.86 t/s   nothing shared
#                      65_536   64.81       attention, not the gate
#                      32_768   66.06       both
#                       8_192   65.85       and the residual joins
#                       2_048   65.80       the same
#
#   The load rose from 1.01 to 2.79 across the sweep, so the later points
#   are the disadvantaged ones and the winner is not the last.
#
#   THREE THINGS BUILT AND REFUSED.
#
#   Packing the activation on the pool. It is 0.33 ms of a token on the
#   submitting task and the largest single thing left after the gate, and
#   it is already a share when there is enough of it. Dropping the bound
#   from 256 blocks to 64 and to 32 reads 65.8 and 65.9 against 66.3: a
#   generated token packs 64 blocks and posting them costs more than doing
#   them.
#
#   Sharing the residual joins. Written as shares, and 2,048 elements
#   against a bound of 32,768 at every width there is a model of here. The
#   two sweep points below the bound admit them and measure no better.
#
#   Sharing the rotation. A profile puts Apply_Rotary at 0.037 ms a token,
#   which is one wake and one barrier.
#
#   AND A TAGGED TYPE DECLARED INSIDE THE LAYER LOOP COSTS 2.3 MICROSECONDS
#   A LAYER TO ELABORATE. Three more empty ones in the same block measured
#   65.79 against 66.44 -- one per cent of the token -- so attention's
#   share is declared once for the whole call now and carries the cache
#   offsets, the window's first position and the sink as fields. A profile
#   said nothing about this; a controlled comparison said all of it.
#
#   RMS_Norm zeroed its target and then wrote every element of it, which is
#   a pass across the activation for nothing, forty-four times a token.
#   Removing it reads inside the spread and it is gone anyway.
#
#   WHAT THE ROUND IS WORTH. Alternated three rounds against three, 128
#   generated, --repack rows, --backend cpu:
#
#     Q4_0     65.12 65.13 65.28  ->  66.11 66.13 66.46   1.015
#     Q4_K_M   53.46 54.06 54.14  ->  54.13 54.67 54.87   1.011
#     Q8_0     38.90 39.02 39.19  ->  39.35 39.43 39.45   1.008
#     q8 1419 prompt  4.504 4.539 4.592 s -> 4.575 4.566 4.561   a wash
#
#   THE PARTITION IS HELD BY THE CONFORMANCE RUN. A share that does half
#   its range fails: the gated one by ten assertions and 245,795 sequences
#   outside tolerance, the added one by sixteen and 329,643. Both were
#   measured by breaking the share on purpose.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS RE-MEASURED. This sitting, both sides through the gate:
#
#     engine 6-tok 12gen cpu     0.342 s -- 0.041 + 0.299, 1.82 s processor
#     engine, --arith f32        1.083 s, 8.66 s processor
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.244 s -- 0.027 + 0.216, 0.09 s processor
#     outside q8 110 cpu         0.282 prompt, 1.625 generating
#     outside q8 1419 cpu        4.538 prompt, 1.783 generating
#     outside q8 110 device      0.070 prompt, 1.217 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.721 prompt, 1.341 generating
#     panels q4_0 110 rows       0.174 prompt, 0.258 for seventeen
#     panels q4km 110 rows       0.213 prompt, 1.178 for sixty-four
#     panels q5km 110 rows       0.231 prompt, 1.365
#     panels q2k 110 rows        0.257 prompt, 0.894
#     drafting q2 12tok          1.319 s; drafted 2.975 s, 24 proposed 7 taken
#     reference 4tok cpu         0.042 + 0.100
#     reference 4tok reference   5.645 + 3.787
#     scaling q8_0 eight shares  14,298 Me/s against 14,436 published
#     kernels q8_0 Row_Dot       0.364 ns an element against 0.37
#     kernels rms norm           1.656 ns an element against 1.676, which
#                                is the zero fill gone and is inside the
#                                spread either way
#     kernels silu               2.117 against 2.126
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1 in the same sitting, through tests outside:
#
#     q8_0 cpu     pp110 396.06  pp1419 305.53  tg64 40.55
#     q8_0 device  pp110 1679.31 pp1419 1892.45 tg64 58.58
#
#   WHICH PUTS BOTH LONG PROMPTS AHEAD -- 312.7 against 305.5 on the
#   processor and 1968.1 against 1892.5 on the device -- and the generated
#   token 1.03 behind, from 1.04 and 1.07 in the two sittings before. The
#   short prompts are 1.02 and 1.07 behind.
#
#   AND THE LARGEST THING LEFT IS ONE LOOP THIS CHANGE MAY NOT TOUCH. The
#   greedy selection walks 32,000 token identifiers, reads two boolean
#   arrays and calls one function that is not inlined, at about thirty
#   cycles each: 0.26 ms of a 15 ms token, the biggest single item on the
#   submitting task now. It is element-wise and would divide perfectly. It
#   is in Model_Runner.Sampling, which decides what a model says and has no
#   business knowing what a host has, so giving it a pool is a seam rather
#   than an optimization and is not in this change.
#
# load:  the gate's own instrument under 1.50 before every reading; the
#        one-minute average is printed beside each and ran 1.7 to 5.1
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q4_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-09, the seam, and the last loop through it.
#
#   The entry above ends by naming what was left: the greedy selection,
#   32,000 token identifiers walked on the task that had just finished
#   waiting for five, and the reason it was left -- it is in
#   Model_Runner.Sampling, which may not depend on a backend.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. Model_Runner.Shares declares two interfaces, Work and
#   Team. The pool wears Team and hands itself out through one function;
#   Backend.CPU.Task_Item is now a name for Shares.Work rather than a second
#   interface saying the same thing; Sampling names Shares and no backend
#   and takes a team as an argument that defaults to none.
#
#   BOTH WALKS ARE REDUCTIONS, WHICH IS WHAT MADE THIS THE HARD ONE. They
#   are cut anyway, and what makes it sound is that no block reduces into a
#   shared place: the vocabulary is divided into a fixed fifty-six blocks,
#   each answers into a slot of its own, and the slots are combined in block
#   order afterwards -- so a tie still goes to the lowest token identifier
#   and the answer does not depend on how many workers a machine has.
#
#   THE BOUND. The greedy walk reads two mask arrays and searches the window
#   the repetition penalty keeps -- about six unpredictable branches an
#   identifier -- so it carries a weight of eight, as the gated middle does.
#   The walk looking for logits that are not numbers carries none and stays
#   below the bound: it is a bit test an element.
#
#   ONE MISTAKE, AND A TEST FOUND IT RATHER THAN A MEASUREMENT. Sharing
#   opened the pool before handing it out. Opening rendezvouses with the
#   workers, and a caller that asks for a team in the same declarative part
#   that declares the pool waits for tasks the language has not activated
#   yet. The suite hung. Opening is left where it was, in the first job
#   posted.
#
#   HELD BY EQUALITY AT EVERY WORKER COUNT. The test samples the same logits
#   with no team and with teams of one through eight and asserts the same
#   token, with the highest value planted twice in blocks a long way apart
#   so that a combination in the wrong order takes the higher identifier;
#   then the same for a logit that is not a number, planted twice, so that
#   both paths must name the first. Mutations, against the sampling case:
#   taking a tie rather than a strict maximum fails two assertions, and
#   every block answering into one slot fails ten.
#
#   AND THE TEST WAS WRONG BEFORE IT WAS RIGHT. It asserted inside the block
#   that declares the pool, so a failing assertion left that frame through
#   finalization while the workers were still waiting -- a hang rather than
#   a failure, and a mutation that hangs is one nobody can read. It gathers
#   now and asserts afterwards, where there is no pool left.
#
#   WHAT IT IS WORTH. Alternated four rounds in both orders, 128 generated,
#   --repack rows, --backend cpu:
#
#     Q4_0     63.99 64.52 64.41 63.10  ->  65.38 65.08 65.04 66.03   1.016
#     Q4_K_M   52.92 52.79 53.31 53.12  ->  53.91 53.90 54.51 55.19   1.022
#     Q8_0     38.44 38.54 38.86 38.59  ->  38.75 38.85 39.09 39.11   1.010
#     q8 1419 prompt  5.298 5.338 5.310 5.398 -> 5.366 5.341 5.432 5.383
#
#   THE ORDER WAS REVERSED ON PURPOSE AND THAT MATTERED. The first three
#   rounds ran the old arm first and showed the prompt slower in the new arm
#   every time; the fourth ran the old arm second and the prompt went the
#   other way at once. A difference that follows the order rather than the
#   code is the machine warming between the arms, and it is the reason the
#   prompt is called a wash rather than a regression.
#
#   A FIFTH ROUND WAS DISCARDED. Its two arms read 5.677 and 4.599 seconds
#   on that prompt, a fifth apart, with the generating rows moving with them
#   -- 37.24 against 39.44 on the eight-bit model. Nothing this repository
#   changed moves a figure by a fifth in ninety seconds.
#
#   SEVEN GROUPS RE-MEASURED. This sitting, both sides through the gate:
#
#     engine 6-tok 12gen cpu     0.340 s -- 0.041 + 0.299, 1.81 s processor
#     engine, --arith f32        1.076 s, 8.60 s processor
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.244 s -- 0.028 + 0.215, 0.14 s processor
#     outside q8 110 cpu         0.282 prompt, 1.610 generating
#     outside q8 1419 cpu        4.528 prompt, 1.763 generating
#     outside q8 110 device      0.072 prompt, 1.196 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.717 prompt, 1.302 generating
#     panels q4_0 110 rows       0.171 prompt, 0.254 for seventeen
#     panels q4km 110 rows       0.219 prompt, 1.164 for sixty-four
#     panels q5km 110 rows       0.231 prompt, 1.340
#     panels q2k 110 rows        0.263 prompt, 0.877
#     drafting q2 12tok          1.308 s; drafted 2.957 s, 24 proposed 7 taken
#     reference 4tok cpu         0.042 + 0.099
#     reference 4tok reference   5.576 + 3.707
#     scaling q8_0 eight shares  14,407 Me/s against 14,298 last sitting
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1 in the same sitting, through tests outside:
#
#     q8_0 cpu     pp110 387.52  pp1419 307.20  tg64 40.56
#     q8_0 device  pp110 1646.90 pp1419 1898.08 tg64 58.22
#
#   WHICH PUTS EVERY PROCESSOR ROW AHEAD BUT THE GENERATED TOKEN, AND THAT
#   ONE WITHIN TWO PER CENT: 390.1 against 387.5 at 110, 313.4 against 307.2
#   at 1419, and 39.8 against 40.6 generating. The gap on a generated token
#   has gone 1.07, 1.04, 1.03, 1.02 across the four sittings of this run of
#   work, while llama.cpp's own 110-token prompt read 397.6, 392.8, 396.1
#   and 387.5 -- which is why the row worth reading is the one that moved
#   against the machine rather than with it.
#
# load:  the gate's own instrument under 1.50 before every reading; the
#        one-minute average is printed beside each and ran 0.7 to 3.5
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q4_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M and Q5_K_M


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-09, a cache that holds only the window.
#
#   Three architectures here slide a window and until now that narrowed what
#   a position could read and nothing else: every layer was given the whole
#   context, including the twenty-two of gemma3's twenty-six that can never
#   look further back than 512 positions. A layer that slides is given the
#   window and a batch now.
#
#     gemma3, 26 layers, 22 windowed at 512, context 32,768
#                                1,834,493,401 -> 332,722,857 bytes   5.51x
#     gemma2, 26 layers, 13 windowed at 4096, context 8192
#                                1,834,377,279 -> 1,433,611,535       1.28x
#
#   The difference between them is the whole shape of it: gemma3's window is
#   a sixty-fourth of its context and gemma2's is half.
#
#   A RING WAS THE OBVIOUS DESIGN AND IS NOT THE ONE HERE. Storing position
#   P at P mod cells keeps the cache full and never copies, and it makes a
#   window's positions non-contiguous -- which every hot loop in attention
#   assumes they are not: Head_Scores_Across is handed a first key and a
#   stride and walks. The window slides instead: what it still needs is
#   moved down to the front when a run passes the end of a layer's room,
#   which is a window of rows every batch of positions -- eight rows for
#   every row written on gemma2, one on gemma3, against the six hundred
#   megabytes of weights that position reads.
#
#   THE SEAM THAT MADE IT SAFE is that the blend is given cells rather than
#   positions: where the rows sit rather than what they are. Every distance
#   it takes is a difference between two of them, so the causal mask, the
#   fall-off with distance and the softmax are none the wiser, and a change
#   to the cache's shape left three attention kernels, both evaluation paths
#   and the device untouched.
#
#   HELD BY THE SAME BYTES AND BY THE INDEPENDENT IMPLEMENTATION. Both
#   windowed models produce the text they produced before, byte for byte, on
#   the 1419-token prompt -- 1328 tokens as gemma3 cuts it, which is past
#   the window and a batch and slides. And a fixture that slides is crossed
#   against the reference transformer at 518 positions.
#
#   THE TEST'S LENGTH IS THE PART WORTH READING. At 560 positions it passes
#   with the move removed: a window of three reaches three positions back,
#   and forty positions after a slide there is nothing of the move left to
#   be wrong about. At 518 the last position reads across the rows that
#   moved and removing the move fails it, as does keeping one row too few.
#   A LONGER TEST WOULD HAVE HELD LESS.
#
#   THE DEVICE KEEPS THE WHOLE CONTEXT and is told so: it holds its own copy
#   of the cache and writes it a position at a time, so a host sliding rows
#   underneath would leave that copy describing positions that have moved.
#
#   THE CHANGE IS A WASH WHERE NO LAYER SLIDES, which is every figure this
#   file publishes -- TinyLlama states no window. Alternated three rounds
#   against three, 128 generated:
#
#     Q4_0     67.44 67.05 67.36  ->  67.54 67.16 67.47
#     Q8_0     39.66 39.53 39.58  ->  39.83 39.73 39.63
#     Q4_K_M   55.75 55.69 55.52  ->  55.68 55.88 55.43
#     q8 1419 prompt  4.543 4.600 4.579 -> 4.500 4.536 4.640
#
#   Overlapping everywhere, which is what one array read instead of a
#   multiply, once a layer a token, should look like.
#
#   SIX GROUPS RE-MEASURED. This sitting, both sides through the gate:
#
#     engine 6-tok 12gen cpu     0.340 s -- 0.041 + 0.297, 1.81 s processor
#     engine, --arith f32        1.083 s, 8.65 s processor
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.245 s -- 0.028 + 0.217, 0.13 s processor
#     outside q8 110 cpu         0.290 prompt, 1.608 generating
#     outside q8 1419 cpu        4.530 prompt, 1.768 generating
#     outside q8 110 device      0.071 prompt, 1.195 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.724 prompt, 1.309 generating
#     panels q4_0 110 rows       0.170 prompt, 0.256 for seventeen
#     panels q4km 110 rows       0.225 prompt, 1.162 for sixty-four
#     panels q5km 110 rows       0.234 prompt, 1.353
#     panels q2k 110 rows        0.260 prompt, 0.879
#     drafting q2 12tok          1.322 s; drafted 2.988 s, 24 proposed 7 taken
#     reference 4tok cpu         0.043 + 0.100
#     reference 4tok reference   5.680 + 3.774
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1 in the same sitting, through tests outside:
#
#     q8_0 cpu     pp110 393.29  pp1419 298.34  tg64 40.62
#     q8_0 device  pp110 1648.96 pp1419 1890.19 tg64 58.49
#
#   Both long prompts ahead -- 313.2 against 298.3 and 1959.9 against 1890.2
#   -- and the generated token 1.02 behind. The short processor prompt reads
#   379.3 against 390.1 last sitting while llama.cpp's reads 393.3 against
#   396.1: a tenth of a second measured twice on a fifteen-watt part.
#
# load:  the gate's own instrument under 1.50 before every reading; the
#        one-minute average is printed beside each and ran 2.3 to 4.4
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q4_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M; gemma2, gemma3


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
#
# 2026-09-09, the device slides too, and no shader changed.
#
#   The entry above windowed the cache on the processor and left the device
#   holding the whole context, because the device keeps its own copy and
#   writes it a position at a time. It slides now, and gemma3 costs
#   332,722,857 bytes on either backend where it cost 1,834,493,401.
#
#   NO SHADER CHANGED, and the reason is the same property the host's blend
#   turned on. Everything a shader does with a position is a difference: the
#   causal mask compares a step against a last, the window is derived as
#   last + 1 - window, the fall-off with distance is abs (step - asking).
#   Subtracting the same origin from both sides of a difference leaves it
#   where it was, so passing cells instead of positions is the whole of what
#   the device needed -- that, and writing the moved rows over.
#
#   THE SLIDE SETTLES WHAT THE DEVICE OWES FIRST. The host's copy is
#   deliberately behind: Settle_Cache runs when something is about to read
#   it rather than at the end of every call, which the entry that built it
#   prices at 1.000 s against 0.705 on a 1419-token device prompt. A slide
#   is one of those somethings. It asks only when a layer is about to slide,
#   so the lazy settle still holds for every call that does not -- about one
#   call in five hundred and twelve.
#
#   A ROUND'S TABLE CARRIES A ROW FOR EVERY LAYER NOW. The shader reads a
#   row's last position and the block it reads out of a table at the end of
#   the cache; a row's last position is a cell now, and a cell differs by
#   layer and -- a round's rows being different sessions -- by row. The
#   whole table goes over once and each layer is handed the offset of its
#   own slice, so this is a longer write rather than a write a layer, and
#   the guard that caps a round is on rows times layers.
#
#   HELD BY THE SAME BYTES, ON BOTH BACKENDS. gemma3 and gemma2 on the
#   1419-token prompt give the digests they gave before, on --backend cpu
#   and on --backend device. A device round on gemma2 -- two members, two
#   callers -- gives the mark it gave before.
#
#   AND ONE THING IS HELD BY CONSTRUCTION RATHER THAN BY A RUN. A round that
#   has SLID would need 515 positions in each of its members, which on
#   gemma2 is 4608 and on the fixtures is more than a round test steps. So
#   the per-row, per-layer origin in that table is exercised with every
#   origin at zero: replacing each row's origin with the first row's gives
#   the same mark, because at that length there is nothing to tell apart.
#   What is held is the shape. What is not held by a run is two members of
#   one round having slid to different places.
#
#   A STRAY GATE RUN SPOILED A WHOLE BATCH OF READINGS AND IS RECORDED. The
#   first attempt at the figures below read the eight-bit floating-point run
#   at 1.284 s against 1.076, the device's twelve tokens at 0.285 against
#   0.245 and the drafted run at 3.362 against 2.988 -- fifteen to
#   twenty-five per cent across the board, with the device's clock at 1732
#   MHz against 2137. A `tests check` left running from an earlier command
#   was on the machine at 110 per cent of a core, and the part was at
#   89.4 C. The load gate reads busy processors and had let every one of
#   those readings through. THE GATE DOES NOT READ A THERMOMETER, and on a
#   fifteen-watt part it should: the readings were taken again at 49.8 C and
#   agree with the sitting before them to a per cent.
#
#   SIX GROUPS RE-MEASURED, on a cool machine, both sides through the gate:
#
#     engine 6-tok 12gen cpu     0.339 s -- 0.042 + 0.297, 1.81 s processor
#     engine, --arith f32        1.081 s, 8.64 s processor
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.247 s -- 0.029 + 0.218, 0.12 s processor
#     outside q8 110 cpu         0.281 prompt, 1.610 generating
#     outside q8 1419 cpu        4.506 prompt, 1.764 generating
#     outside q8 110 device      0.072 prompt, 1.182 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.711 prompt, 1.299 generating
#     panels q4_0 110 rows       0.166 prompt, 0.254 for seventeen
#     panels q4km 110 rows       0.211 prompt, 1.148 for sixty-four
#     panels q5km 110 rows       0.224 prompt, 1.330
#     panels q2k 110 rows        0.259 prompt, 0.872
#     drafting q2 12tok          1.318 s; drafted 2.968 s, 24 proposed 7 taken
#     reference 4tok cpu         0.041 + 0.099
#     reference 4tok reference   5.576 + 3.748
#
#   llama-bench at 95b8e33e1 in the same sitting, through tests outside:
#
#     q8_0 cpu     pp110 383.87  pp1419 298.59  tg64 40.59
#     q8_0 device  pp110 1637.66 pp1419 1908.43 tg64 58.24
#
#   WHICH PUTS EVERY PROCESSOR ROW AHEAD BUT THE GENERATED TOKEN: 391.5
#   against 383.9 at 110, 314.9 against 298.6 at 1419, and 39.8 against 40.6
#   generating, which is 1.02 behind. The device's long prompt is ahead too,
#   1995.8 against 1908.4.
#
# load:  the gate's own instrument under 1.50 before every reading, and the
#        part under 50 C at the start of each batch
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0, Q4_0, Q2_K, Q4_K_M, Q5_K_M; gemma2, gemma3

# 2026-09-09, the thermometer the gate reads and does not refuse on, and the
# bound that came down to a twentieth of the machine.
#
#   WHAT THE ENTRY ABOVE ASKED FOR, and what measuring it said instead. That
#   entry ends "THE GATE DOES NOT READ A THERMOMETER, and on a fifteen-watt
#   part it should", on the strength of one spoiled batch taken at 89.4 C
#   with a stray `tests check` on the machine. Two things were true of that
#   batch and only one of them was the cause.
#
#   THE HEAT CURVE REFUTES THE TEMPERATURE. The same 1419-token prompt,
#   eighteen times back to back, the part climbing as it went:
#
#     reading   processor   device   load   prompt
#        1        50.1 C    47.0 C   0.36   4.167 s
#        3        81.0      59.0     1.69   4.485
#        7        84.0      63.0     3.68   4.512
#       12        86.0      66.0     5.31   4.526
#       18        87.0      68.0     6.25   4.540
#
#   From 78 degrees upward every reading is 4.50 to 4.61 -- FLAT OVER NINE
#   DEGREES, and 87 C is where this part sits after a minute of work, which
#   is to say where nearly every figure in this file was taken. There is no
#   temperature here at which a figure stops being worth publishing, and a
#   bound invented for one would have refused readings that were fine.
#
#   What the curve does say is that reading 1 is 8 per cent fast. A COLD
#   PART FLATTERS, once, and that is an argument for taking a batch warm or
#   dropping its first reading -- not for a gate.
#
#   THE BUSY CORE IS THE WHOLE OF IT. One spinner, alternated against
#   nothing, three rounds:
#
#     round   alone     one core busy
#       1     4.130 s      5.078 s
#       2     4.484       5.330
#       3     4.527       5.362
#
#   NINETEEN PER CENT. And what the gate's own instrument read while that
#   spinner ran: 1.05 busy processors, against a bound of 1.50. Idle reads
#   0.07 and two spinners read 2.08, so the instrument is right and the
#   bound was wrong -- 1.50 admits a busy processor and a fifth of a
#   figure, which is exactly what got published and thrown away.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. Host_Load.Warmth: the highest of the processor and
#   device sensors under /sys/class/hwmon, in degrees, negative where the
#   host has none. Carried beside the load by `tests speed` and `tests
#   benchmark` and refused on by neither, which is the measurement above
#   written into the program. Host_Load.Processors, counting cpuN in
#   /proc/stat: 16 here. And Too_Busy, which was the constant 1.50 and is
#   now a twentieth of the machine and never below a quarter of a processor
#   -- 0.80 here, under the 1.05 one spinner reads and well above the 0.07
#   this machine idles at.
#
#   THE CHECK. Two, and both fail on the mistake they are for: a bound at
#   or above one busy processor on a host of twenty processors or fewer,
#   and a thermometer answering something no working part is at, which is
#   what a reader that forgets sysfs counts thousandths would answer. The
#   gate was then run: quiet, it published; with one spinner it refused,
#   naming 1.00 busy processors against the 0.80.
#
#   EIGHT GROUPS RESTAMPED, NOT RE-MEASURED. What changed in speed_run.adb
#   is two calls to Warmth and a clause in the summary line; in
#   benchmarks.adb, a clause in the line after the last run. Both calls sit
#   outside every clock -- the first before the model is opened, the second
#   after everything is closed -- so nothing between the clocks moved, and
#   the header's second choice is the one this earns. Every figure in the
#   entry above stands, and was taken four days before this on the same
#   tree minus these two clauses.
#
# load:  0.16 quiet, 1.00 with the spinner that the gate refused
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0

# 2026-09-09, the device was not sliding, and three lines said so.
#
#   WHAT THE LAST TWO ENTRIES BUILT AND WHAT RAN. The cache was narrowed on
#   the processor, then "the device slides too, and no shader changed" said
#   the same of the device. It did not. `Open` decided a layer's cells
#   under a condition that read `Settings.Window > 0 and then Source.Able
#   .Kind /= Backend_Device`, so on the device every layer got the whole
#   context, nothing slid, and the shader arguments that entry changed were
#   handed an origin that stayed zero. WHICH IS WHY THE DIGESTS MATCHED AND
#   NO FIGURE MOVED. The evidence that would have caught it is the memory,
#   and that entry did not take it.
#
#   PEAK RESIDENT, gemma3 and gemma2 at their own contexts, one token:
#
#                          before      after
#     gemma3 processor    1,163,504   1,163,504 kB
#     gemma3 device       2,616,824   1,220,192
#     gemma2 device       3,493,052   3,120,228
#
#   1.40 GB the device was carrying that this file said it was not, and the
#   processor's column unchanged, which is what says the fix is the device's
#   geometry and not something else.
#
#   AND A SECOND LINE UNDER IT. Take_Block numbers seats from zero and tests
#   `Seat >= 0` for whether a session holds one; the slide's write to the
#   device tested `Seat > 0`. A lone session always gets seat zero, so a
#   slide's moved rows never reached the device. Harmless while nothing
#   slid, a wrong answer once the geometry was corrected: the two are one
#   fix or neither is.
#
#   AND THE ROLLING CONTEXT, which raised. Shift found both ends of its move
#   through a position's distance from the lowest one its layer still holds,
#   and the positions a shift keeps are the first ones a window drops, so
#   that distance went below zero. --context-shift raised on gemma2, gemma3
#   and gpt-oss for any context long enough to have slid.
#
#   WHAT HOLDS IT. The conformance sweep on both backends -- 41,780
#   sequences, none outside tolerance -- and the fixture check across the
#   windowed architectures on BACKEND_DEVICE. Plus a new test that shifts a
#   cache which has slid and requires the answer twenty positions later to
#   equal, TO THE BIT, a run that never read what was dropped: two layers of
#   a window of three reach four positions back, so nothing before the shift
#   can touch that logit. Its second assertion takes the token immediately
#   after the shift and holds it to 1e-3, which is what the turn-back is
#   held by; without it, mutating the turn-back to zero passes.
#
#   WHAT IS NOT HELD BY A RUN: the device's copy after a Shift. The tiny
#   fixture never takes a device block -- Whole_Layer_Fits and its
#   neighbours turn it down at that size, the seat stays -1 -- so removing
#   that write leaves every test passing. Said here rather than left to be
#   discovered the way the three above were.
#
#   SIX GROUPS RESTAMPED, NOT RE-MEASURED. Every published figure is
#   TinyLlama, which declares no window: Windowed is false either way, so
#   the geometry is the geometry it was; Make_Room returns at its first line
#   when Window is zero, so the seat fix cannot be reached; and no speed run
#   calls Shift. Nothing a published figure runs through changed.
#
# load:  0.16 to 0.60 through the gate; the part at 47 to 82 C
# model: gemma3, gemma2 for the memory; TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0 elsewhere

# 2026-09-09, drafting out of the context, and a penalty that made every
# drafted run wrong.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. Model_Runner.Lookup: the last two tokens of what a run
#   has said, found where they last occurred, and what followed them
#   proposed. --draft-lookup on the command and on tests speed. The
#   proposals go through the machinery --draft-model already used, so the
#   only new code is the search and the buffer it searches.
#
#   THE FIGURES. A 206-token prompt asking the model to repeat a passage it
#   was just given; 128 tokens; alternated against no drafting, three rounds
#   each, both sides through the gate; generating time alone:
#
#     processor   plain 3.249 3.269 3.277   lookup 2.131 2.175 2.179   1.51x
#     device      plain 2.409 2.414 2.421   lookup 1.469 1.483 1.480   1.63x
#
#   116 proposed and 69 accepted, on both backends and in every round --
#   the proposals are a function of the text and not of the machine, which
#   is why the two backends agree on them exactly. THE DIGEST IS
#   b0fa9fb7d8535607 IN ALL TWELVE READINGS.
#
#   HOW MANY TO PROPOSE, same run at three depths:
#
#     a round   processor   device   proposed / accepted
#        4       2.184 s    1.528 s      116 / 69
#        8       2.276      1.547        176 / 75
#       16       2.633      1.549        284 / 87
#
#   Four on the processor, flat on the device, so --draft-tokens keeps the
#   default it had. A deeper draft is righter in total and costs more than
#   that is worth where a batch row is real arithmetic.
#
#   AND WHAT IT COSTS WITH NOTHING TO LOOK UP, which is the figure that says
#   whether to leave the flag on. Sixteen tokens after a seventeen-token
#   prompt, nothing proposed at all: 0.396 s plain against 0.420 -- six per
#   cent. NOT THE SEARCH: with the search replaced by "propose nothing" the
#   run still read 0.422. It is the drafting round, which --draft-model has
#   always paid too. A round that proposes nothing now evaluates its one
#   token the ordinary way instead of as a batch of one, which took this
#   from fifteen per cent to six; the rest is the round's bookkeeping and is
#   the next thing to look at here.
#
#   A REPETITION PENALTY MADE EVERY DRAFTED RUN WRONG, and had since
#   drafting was written. A penalty reads the tokens said so far; a round
#   samples several positions before it emits any of them, so every row was
#   scored against the history as it stood when the round began. Through the
#   command, qwen3 at --repeat-penalty 1.1, 48 tokens:
#
#     before   plain and --draft-model differed
#     after    identical, at --draft-tokens 1 and at 4
#
#   At --repeat-penalty 1.0 they always agreed, which is why this survived:
#   the test that holds the guarantee uses the greedy configuration, whose
#   penalty is 1.0, and the command's default is 1.1.
#
#   ONE THING LEFT OPEN when this entry was first written, and closed the
#   same day. Through `tests speed` --draft-model still read a different
#   digest from plain at 1.1 where --draft-lookup agreed. IT IS NOT
#   DRAFTING. A drafted run's tokens come out of a batch and an undrafted
#   run's out of single evaluations, and those are not the same arithmetic:
#
#     qwen3, the same prompt, NO DRAFTING AT ALL
#       --batch-size 1     4c7c64cc5dbd3aa7
#       --batch-size 2     693f988b17fd427c
#       --batch-size 8     693f988b17fd427c
#       --batch-size 512   693f988b17fd427c
#
#   The same sweep on TinyLlama and the quoting prompt gives one digest at
#   all three sizes, so this is a nearly tied position rather than a
#   systematic difference. The size of the difference: the first generated
#   token of that prompt reads a log probability of -1.815483 read a token
#   at a time and -1.831034 read in batches -- the same token either way,
#   and some later position was closer than 0.0155.
#
#   So the guarantee drafting makes is exact UP TO the difference between
#   the batched and the single-token paths. Both are held against the
#   independent implementation inside the conformance tolerance and neither
#   is held against the other, which is the right arrangement and a smaller
#   claim than the README made.
#
#   SIX GROUPS RESTAMPED, NOT RE-MEASURED. Nothing a published figure runs
#   through changed: Drafting is false without --draft-tokens, the lookup is
#   reached only when Draft_From_Context is set, and the one change on a
#   shared path -- Record_Token moving into the round -- is inside
#   `if not Drafting`, which every published figure is.
#
# load:  0.49 to 1.09 through the gate; the part at 55 to 82 C
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0; qwen3 for the penalty check

# 2026-09-09, a served caller keeps what its prompt shares with the one
# before it.
#
#   WHAT IT COST NOT TO. A seat's session outlives the member in it and was
#   reset between callers, so every arrival read its whole prompt again.
#   Eight seats, the 208-token quoting prompt, thirty-two tokens a caller:
#
#     callers   rounds   wall
#        8        35     5.844 s
#       16        61    11.562
#       32       122    23.380
#
#   Dead linear at 0.73 s a caller, of which 0.53 is 208 tokens at the
#   processor's own 390 a second. The same benchmark with a five-token
#   prompt runs in 2.742 s, so three quarters of that wall was a prompt read
#   again.
#
#   WHAT IT COSTS NOW, alternated against --no-reuse:
#
#     callers   plain                    keeping                  kept
#        8      5.818 6.139 6.250 s      5.996 6.151 6.222 s         0
#       16     11.801                    7.410            1.59x   1656
#       32     23.488                   10.047            2.34x   4968
#
#   The prompt is read once a seat rather than once a caller, so the saving
#   is the ratio of the two: nothing when they are equal, and three
#   alternated rounds there are inside each other's spread, which is what
#   says the comparison and the seat choice cost nothing measurable.
#
#   THE MARK MOVES AND THAT IS NOT THE REUSE BEING WRONG. A caller that kept
#   207 positions reads its last one alone where a caller that kept nothing
#   read it in a stretch, and the entry before this one measured what that
#   costs: batched and single evaluation are not bit-for-bit the same
#   arithmetic. The server already had the property -- the stretch is
#   divided among the members still reading, so how much of a prompt goes
#   into one pass already depends on who else arrived.
#
#   WHAT IS EXACT is that rewinding and reading a token again answers what
#   it answered the first time, to the bit, which is the whole of what the
#   reuse rests on and has a test of its own.
#
#   THE FLOOR A SLIDING WINDOW PUTS UNDER IT. Llama.Reusable_From: a layer
#   that has slid holds the newest positions and no others, so an agreement
#   below its floor is refused and the prompt read from nothing. Without it
#   a 518-token caller followed by one sharing eight tokens refuses the
#   round with INTERNAL_INVARIANT_VIOLATED, which is the mutation the test
#   for it fails on.
#
#   SIX GROUPS RESTAMPED, NOT RE-MEASURED. Nothing a published figure runs
#   through changed: no published figure serves, Reusable_From is called
#   only from the server, and speed_run.adb gained a parameter and a clause
#   in a line the serving summary prints.
#
# load:  under 0.60 through the gate; the part at 60 to 80 C
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0

# 2026-09-09, what a format costs the predictions, and a projection done a
# position at a time.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. tests perplexity: a corpus scored a chunk at a time, the
#   second half of each chunk counted, and against an --against model of the
#   same weights in another format, the Kullback-Leibler divergence from its
#   distributions. llama.cpp's llama-perplexity, and the half of this
#   repository's format table that was asserted rather than measured.
#
#   TWELVE FORMATS OF TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat, 1,020 scored positions each,
#   against the eight-bit file, ordered by what they cost:
#
#     format    perplexity   divergence   worst    top token
#     q8_0        35.5256     0.000000    0.0000     100.0 %
#     q5_k_m      35.6834     0.010226    0.1492      93.8
#     q5_1        35.7907     0.013021    0.2483      91.8
#     q5_0        35.8219     0.013543    0.1043      93.0
#     iq4_nl      36.5197     0.035287    0.7945      87.6
#     iq4_xs      36.4245     0.036373    0.7856      86.7
#     q4_k_m      36.6017     0.044413    0.4610      87.9
#     q4_0        36.1500     0.051893    0.8296      87.0
#     q4_1        37.7095     0.060082    1.1356      85.9
#     q3_k        37.0745     0.091220    0.9899      82.3
#     mxfp4       38.3502     0.120443    1.8979      79.6
#     q2_k        45.0612     0.289359    3.4884      71.0
#
#   THE FIRST ROW IS THE CHECK: the eight-bit file against itself reads
#   exactly zero and agrees with itself everywhere.
#
#   MXFP4 IS THE FINDING. A four-bit format between q3_k and q2_k, twice
#   q3_k's divergence, a different top token on a fifth of the positions.
#   Its scale is one byte holding a power of two where every other four-bit
#   format here carries a half-precision one, and the support matrix
#   described the layout and said nothing about what it costs.
#
#   AND THE DIVERGENCE IS THE COLUMN TO READ: q4_0 has a lower perplexity
#   than q4_1 and q4_k_m and a higher divergence than both. Perplexity is a
#   fact about the model and these thousand positions; divergence is a fact
#   about the format.
#
#   WHAT BOUNDS IT. The baseline is q8_0 and not f16, because no
#   half-precision file of this model is here. The corpus is a fixed
#   15,440-token fixture of this repository's own prose, because the
#   alternative was carrying wikitext. Both are the price of measuring
#   rather than asserting, and neither number is comparable outside this
#   repository. THE FIRST CORPUS TRIED was speed-prompt-long.txt, which is
#   nine distinct lines repeated ten times and scored a perplexity of 1.27 --
#   the model reciting. Worth knowing about a file several published speed
#   figures are taken on.
#
#   AND A PROJECTION DONE A POSITION AT A TIME. Evaluate_Batch fills a
#   caller's every-position logits by pushing each position through the
#   output matrix separately, where the round path below it had always
#   batched the same work: sixty-five megabytes read per position rather
#   than once for the batch. One matrix product now.
#
#     the perplexity measurement   281 s -> 9.9 s   (with the worker pool
#                                                   the tool also lacked)
#
#   EVERY DRAFTED FIGURE MOVED, so they are re-measured here rather than
#   restamped. Alternated, three rounds each, generating time alone:
#
#     lookup, quoting prompt, processor
#       plain  3.250 3.275 3.279     lookup 1.983 2.019 2.009   1.63x
#     lookup, quoting prompt, device
#       plain  2.406 2.383 2.392     lookup 1.338 1.334 1.352   1.78x
#
#   which read 1.51x and 1.63x before. b0fa9fb7d8535607 in all twelve.
#
#     a round proposing   processor   device
#            4            1.964 s     1.330 s
#            8            1.998       1.328
#           16            2.157       1.207
#
#   THE TWO BACKENDS NOW WANT DIFFERENT DEPTHS. Four is still best on the
#   processor; sixteen is nine per cent better than four on the device,
#   where the curve used to be flat. The default stays at four.
#
#     twelve tokens, medians of three
#       q8_0                       0.340 s   (was 0.339)
#       q2_k                       1.401     (was 1.318, and this pair's
#                                             ratio has wandered for a year)
#       q8_0 drafted by q2_k       2.919     (was 2.968), 24 proposed 7 taken
#
#   THE CHECK OF FIVE DRAFTED POSITIONS: 2.617 s of generating, less the
#   draft's 24 x 95 ms = 2.28, leaves 0.34 for six checks -- 57 ms, against
#   98 before, and against 25 ms for one token generated normally. The
#   conclusion is unchanged and its margin is not: the check used to cost
#   twice what a round's tokens are worth and now costs about the same.
#
#   FOUR GROUPS RESTAMPED AND TWO RE-MEASURED. The drafting figures moved
#   and are above. Everything else runs through Evaluate_Batch with no Every
#   asked for, where the changed branch is not reached.
#
# load:  through the gate before every reading; the part at 54 to 93 C, and
#        the twelve-format sweep ran warm throughout, which the entry of
#        2026-09-09 on the thermometer says is where this part sits
# model: TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat in twelve formats

# 2026-09-09, writing a format, and the check that it is the format.
#
#   WHAT WAS BUILT. tests quantize: a model read and written out again in
#   another format. llama.cpp's llama-quantize, for the five whose encoding
#   is a rule rather than a search -- Q8_0, Q4_0, Q4_1, Q5_0, Q5_1.
#
#   THE ENCODERS WERE HERE AND THE RULE WAS NOT. Fixtures.Encode_Four_Bit
#   serves both Q4_0 and Q4_1 by scaling a block from its minimum and its
#   maximum, which is the reference rule for Q4_1 and is not the rule for
#   Q4_0: that one tracks the largest magnitude and keeps its sign, so a
#   block of positive weights takes a NEGATIVE scale. Nothing noticed for as
#   long as there have been encoders here, because the fixture check decodes
#   what an encoder wrote and holds the decoder.
#
#   THE CHECK IS EXACT AND IS THE OTHER IMPLEMENTATION. Both pointed at the
#   same eight-bit file, compared tensor by tensor:
#
#     format   same   differing   absent
#      q8_0     201       0         0
#      q4_0     201       0         0
#      q4_1     201       0         0
#      q5_0     201       0         0
#      q5_1     201       0         0
#
#   Every tensor of every format, byte for byte, including the parts of the
#   rule that look like mistakes and are not -- the truncation toward zero
#   after adding a half, and the clamp on the high side only. The two q4_0
#   files come to the same 620,803,456 bytes, and the one this wrote answers
#   a prompt the way the one llama.cpp wrote does.
#
#   llama-quantize was given --allow-requantize, because it refuses an
#   eight-bit source otherwise, and --pure, because by default it holds the
#   output projection and the token embedding above the name on the file
#   where this tool converts everything that fills whole blocks.
#
#   AND THE QUALITY GROUP RESTAMPED, NOT RE-MEASURED. perplexity_run.adb is
