#   tokens routed to them, so a fresh process generated its first hundred
#   tokens at 7.98 t/s while five gigabytes crossed a few matrices at a
#   time. Products.Hold is Acquire without the dispatch, and Prepare holds
#   every stack where the model is Stacked: the same eleven gigabytes
#   cross once, during the load, and the six-token prompt above generates
#   at 22 from its first token. A dense model is untouched -- its first
#   token uploads everything anyway -- and the load of the mixture takes
#   the seconds the first tokens took.
#
#   FIVE GROUPS RESTAMPED AND NONE RE-MEASURED: quality, engine,
#   reference, drafting and panels name model_runner-llama.adb, and what
#   changed in it is a loop that runs only where a device holds a
#   mixture's stacks; no processor path moved. The device and outside
#   groups name the shaders and the engine and were re-measured:
#
#     device 6-tok 12gen         0.250 s -- 0.027 + 0.224, digest unchanged
#     outside q8 110 device      0.067 prompt, 1.205 generating
#     outside q8 1419 device     0.712 prompt, 1.327 generating
#
#   against 0.249 (0.025 + 0.223), 0.068 / 1.209 and 0.724 / 1.343 the
#   sitting before, and the processor rows of the outside group ran no
#   changed code.
#
# load:  through the gate before every reading; the mixture rows are
#        ungated CLI runs, warm, alternated
# model: Qwen3-30B-A3B Q2_K, Qwen3-8B Q4_K_M, TinyLlama-1.1B-Chat Q8_0

# 2026-09-11, a batch's mixture shared by expert on the pool, the rows
# packed once, and one submission a token priced and not built.
#
#   THE PROCESSOR'S PROMPT on a mixture read 71 tokens a second against
#   llama.cpp's 108 at 110 tokens, and the reading was that an expert's
#   product -- a few vectors by 768 rows -- was cut across eight workers
#   for a share of a few hundred rows each, three hundred times a layer.
#
#   BUILT: an expert to a worker. Mixture_Batch on the pool posts one job
#   a layer through Dispatch_Shares, and each worker gathers, multiplies,
#   gates and scatters its own experts whole, serially, into the places
#   the ranks name. The experts are dealt into the shares largest first,
#   back and forth, so a share has the same number of experts as the next
#   and about the same rows. A WASH: 68.7 70.9 72.7 against 73.3 65.0
#   68.1 alternated on the 110-token prompt, which is inside a spread that
#   the eleven-gigabyte panel copy every run makes wide. The per-product
#   wake was not where the time was.
#
#   THEN THE QUANTIZATION. A product quantizes its activation first, at
#   about two nanoseconds an element, and a mixture's prompt hands every
#   row to eight experts and to a gate and an up each: the same row packed
#   sixteen times a layer. Workers_CPU.Pack quantizes the layer's rows
#   once, for each kind of sums the layer's formats want, and
#   Multiply_Packed gathers an expert's members out of the packed batch
#   and multiplies them serially. Alternated on the 1419-token prompt,
#   where the copy's noise is amortized:
#
#     packed once   68.58 68.65 t/s
#     by row        65.58 65.72
#
#   Four and a half per cent, kept. The bits are the bits: the products
#   are the products the pool cut, and the same 24-token text comes back.
#
#   AND THE COMPARISON THAT MATTERS NOW. llama-bench's pp1419 on the
#   processor for this file is 250.5 t/s, so at a long prompt this program
#   is 3.6 behind where at 110 tokens it is 1.5: llama.cpp's rate rises
#   with the members an expert gets and this one's does not. A dense
#   prompt here reads 440 t/s on TinyLlama, which is 4.8e11 multiply-adds
#   a second; the mixture reads 2.3e11 at eighty members an expert and
#   the same at seven. Whatever holds it flat is in the strip kernel at
#   768 rows and 2048 or 768 columns -- a per-call cost that a
#   5632-row matrix amortizes and a 768-row one does not -- and that is
#   a `tests benchmark` question at those shapes, not answered here.
#
#   ONE SUBMISSION A TOKEN, PRICED AND NOT BUILT. The heads entry above
#   measured a dispatch between dispatches at about 1.5 microseconds; a
#   submission is a queue call, a fence reset and a semaphore, tens of
#   microseconds, and there are forty-eight a token where there were nine
#   hundred and sixty dispatches: at most two or three milliseconds of a
#   forty-five millisecond token, on the same reasoning that priced the
#   dispatches at twelve and got 0.3. The log's own reading at twenty-two
#   layers was a wash, and building it means a descriptor bank a layer.
#   Not worth the machinery for a bound that small.
#
#   GROUPS. The processor's mixture prompt runs code no published figure
#   runs; the device did not change; the scaling group names the pool,
#   whose products are untouched by two operations added beside them.
#   Restamped, none re-measured.
#
# load:  ungated CLI runs, warm, alternated; the one-minute average 2 to 4
# model: Qwen3-30B-A3B Q2_K

# 2026-09-11, the plan's first step: the expert shapes measured in the
# tool, the pool's idle half found, and the tokens-tiled thesis refuted
# before it was built.
#
#   docs/plan-mixture.md, step 0: `tests benchmark` now measures the
#   pool's product at a mixture's expert shapes -- 768 by 2048, 2048 by
#   768, and the dense 5632 by 2048 beside them -- in the panelled k-quants
#   at 1, 8, 32 and 80 vectors, in the arithmetic a run uses. Nanoseconds
#   a multiply-add on the pool:
#
#     q2_k  768 x 2048    1: 0.00487   8: 0.00226   32: 0.00198   80: 0.00209
#     q2_k 2048 x  768       0.00493      0.00265       0.00209       0.00204
#     q2_k 5632 x 2048       0.00338      0.00197       0.00188       0.00188
#     q3_k  768 x 2048       0.00611      0.00253       0.00225       0.00216
#     q4_k  768 x 2048       0.00524      0.00222       0.00192       0.00187
#     q4_k 5632 x 2048       0.00350      0.00176       0.00167       0.00166
#
#   TWO THINGS THE PLAN ASSUMED ARE NOT SO. The cost an element is the
#   same at 768 rows as at 5632, within a tenth: no per-call cost that a
#   small matrix fails to amortize. And it is flat from eight vectors to
#   eighty because the kernel is at its arithmetic's rate there -- 0.0019
#   ns a multiply-add is 5.3e11 a second on eight cores, 66 G a core,
#   which is the dense prompt's rate exactly. A wider tile would amortize
#   a decode that is not the cost. What llama.cpp has at 250 tokens a
#   second on the 1419-token prompt is 8e11 and more a second FROM THE
#   SAME INSTRUCTIONS' PEAK: their kernel spends fewer instructions a
#   multiply-add, which the log already knew of the strip kernel -- 39
#   instructions for 256 where the byte dot product's floor is 8. Step 1
#   is that, not a tile.
#
#   THE FIRST TRY AT THE TOOL MEASURED THE WRONG ARITHMETIC: the benchmark
#   command leaves the pool in the binary32 path, and the first readings
#   were twenty times slower and consistent with nothing -- 0.04 ns a
#   multiply-add, eight cores of decode-and-dot. The measure sets the
#   quantized path for itself now, as the token budget does.
#
#   AND THE POOL WAS HALF IDLE. Forty samples of every thread during the
#   1419-token prompt: as many workers waiting for a share as in a kernel.
#   The experts were dealt to the shares whole, and one chosen by eighty
#   positions beside one chosen by two is a share four times another
#   however they are dealt. The job's items are now runs of at most
#   sixteen members of an expert, which is about the same work each, and
#   the shares are level to a run:
#
#     110-token prompt    80.08 77.25 t/s   against 71 before the runs
#     1419-token prompt   71.37 61.80       against 68.98 68.93
#
#   The short prompt gains an eighth; the long one is inside a spread the
#   panel copy keeps wide. Kept. What the long prompt's samples also show
#   is attention on the host -- head_scores_across, a sixth of the
#   kernel's samples at 1302 positions -- and the router's product on the
#   binary32 path, the router not being panelled.
#
#   GROUPS. The processor's mixture prompt and the benchmark tool changed;
#   no published figure runs either -- the kernels group names the tool,
#   whose row-dot measure is untouched by a section added before it.
#   Restamped, none re-measured.
#
# load:  ungated CLI runs, warm, alternated; the one-minute average 2 to 4
# model: Qwen3-30B-A3B Q2_K
